# Proverbs

## Proverbs 1  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-02

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17141

<!-- post 17141 · 2026-02-02 05:55 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 1**
*The proverbs of Solomon the son of David, king of Israel;*

*2 To know wisdom and instruction; to perceive the words of understanding;*

*3 To receive the instruction of wisdom, justice, and judgment, and equity;*

*4 To give subtilty to the simple, to the young man knowledge and discretion.*

*5 A wise man will hear, and will increase learning; and a man of understanding shall attain unto wise counsels:*

*6 To understand a proverb, and the interpretation; the words of the wise, and their dark sayings.*

*7 The fear of the Lord is the beginning of knowledge: but fools despise wisdom and instruction.*

*8 My son, hear the instruction of thy father, and forsake not the law of thy mother:*

*9 For they shall be an ornament of grace unto thy head, and chains about thy neck.*

*10 My son, if sinners entice thee, consent thou not.*

*11 If they say, Come with us, let us lay wait for blood, let us lurk privily for the innocent without cause:*

*12 Let us swallow them up alive as the grave; and whole, as those that go down into the pit:*

*13 We shall find all precious substance, we shall fill our houses with spoil:*

*14 Cast in thy lot among us; let us all have one purse:*

*15 My son, walk not thou in the way with them; refrain thy foot from their path:*

*16 For their feet run to evil, and make haste to shed blood.*

*17 Surely in vain the net is spread in the sight of any bird.*

*18 And they lay wait for their own blood; they lurk privily for their own lives.*

*19 So are the ways of every one that is greedy of gain; which taketh away the life of the owners thereof.*

*20 Wisdom crieth without; she uttereth her voice in the streets:*

*21 She crieth in the chief place of concourse, in the openings of the gates: in the city she uttereth her words, saying,*

*22 How long, ye simple ones, will ye love simplicity? and the scorners delight in their scorning, and fools hate knowledge?*

*23 Turn you at my reproof: behold, I will pour out my spirit unto you, I will make known my words unto you.*

*24 Because I have called, and ye refused; I have stretched out my hand, and no man regarded;*

*25 But ye have set at nought all my counsel, and would none of my reproof:*

*26 I also will laugh at your calamity; I will mock when your fear cometh;*

*27 When your fear cometh as desolation, and your destruction cometh as a whirlwind; when distress and anguish cometh upon you.*

*28 Then shall they call upon me, but I will not answer; they shall seek me early, but they shall not find me:*

*29 For that they hated knowledge, and did not choose the fear of the Lord:*

*30 They would none of my counsel: they despised all my reproof.*

*31 Therefore shall they eat of the fruit of their own way, and be filled with their own devices.*

*32 For the turning away of the simple shall slay them, and the prosperity of fools shall destroy them.*

*33 But whoso hearkeneth unto me shall dwell safely, and shall be quiet from fear of evil.*

<!-- post 17142 · 2026-02-02 06:06 · study -->

The book of Proverbs is given to man, a gift of God with a very specific purpose. 

*To know wisdom and instruction; to perceive the words of understanding; To receive the instruction of wisdom, justice, and judgment, and equity; To give subtilty to the simple, to the young man knowledge and discretion.*

Much of the book of wisdom is by Solomon, who God gave an abundance of wisdom and knowledge and understanding, who as King displayed this wisdom in ruling, making Israel great again. But in his personal life, not so much. 

It is astounding how we, as men, can hear God's word, know His word, study it, hear it preached, and yet still lean on our own understanding, rather than in all our ways acknowledging Him that He may direct our paths. 

*A wise man will hear, and will increase learning; and a man of understanding shall attain unto wise counsels: To understand a proverb, and the interpretation; the words of the wise, and their dark sayings.The fear of the Lord is the beginning of knowledge: but fools despise wisdom and instruction.*

God's word should lead us to fear Him. To respect His holiness, power, mercy and justice. His wrath and His judgments. This is what makes a man wise, our fearful respect for the Lord, and being doers of the word, increases our knowledge and faith. 

Solomon writes, *My son, hear the instruction of thy father, and forsake not the law of thy mother,* these truths are from God, and it is every parent's responsibility to teach them to our children, that they be an ornament of grace upon their head, and chains about their necks. It is the greatest inheritance we can give our children. It is also a command: Raising them in the fear and knowledge of God, to bring *them up in the nurture and admonition of the Lord.*

<!-- post 17146 · 2026-02-03 06:11 · study -->

Continuing **Proverbs 1**, we see a warning against joining in another man's sin. Not being led to robbery and violence by greed. That ultimately, it ends in their own blood and death. Crime doesn't pay. 

Next, Solomon tells us of Wisdom, as a woman, though this is more the feminine of the word than a gendered being, and Wisdom is much like the Spirit of God, today. 

The original Hebrew word for wisdom, *hokhmah*, is a feminine noun, and Solomon will compare the virtuous woman of Wisdom to the harlot, the "strange woman," the folly of fools later in the book. 

But the literary device aside, like the male Spirit of God, He reproves and reproaches, and calls men to truth. Like God has done through the inspired word of the prophets, crying out, how long, ye simple ones? Scorners and fools reproofed. 

Wisdom is "poured out" in spirit. Calling us to repentance. God's words, the wisdom itself, made known unto us. As God promises to write the law in our inward parts. 

This echos so many passages, especially when Jesus rebukes the Jews; 

**Matthew 23:37** *O Jerusalem, Jerusalem, thou that killest the prophets, and stonest them which are sent unto thee, how often would I have gathered thy children together, even as a hen gathereth her chickens under her wings, and ye would not!*

**Isaiah 65:2** *I have spread out my hands all the day unto a rebellious people, which walketh in a way that was not good, after their own thoughts*

Those thoughts are foolishness. They set at naught His counsel. This crooked generation refused to accept His Wisdom, and so, He will laugh at their calamity. He will mock the. When their fear cometh. 

What a sobering thing to be mocked of the Lord. To get to the place where you've been given up and given over. When you are destroyed in His presence, cast into eternal torment, and God will laugh, because He gave you ample warning. The wrath of God being revealed from Heaven, that you had no excuse. But they didn't wish to retain God in their knowledge. 

*Then shall they call upon me, but I will not answer; they shall seek me early, but they shall not find me: For that they hated knowledge, and did not choose the fear of the Lord*

But those who fear the Lord, and receive wisdom from Him, have a better promise;

*But whoso hearkeneth unto me shall dwell safely, and shall be quiet from fear of evil*

## Proverbs 2  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-04

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17147

<!-- post 17147 · 2026-02-04 05:48 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 2**
*My son, if thou wilt receive my words, and hide my commandments with thee;*

*2 So that thou incline thine ear unto wisdom, and apply thine heart to understanding;*

*3 Yea, if thou criest after knowledge, and liftest up thy voice for understanding;*

*4 If thou seekest her as silver, and searchest for her as for hid treasures;*

*5 Then shalt thou understand the fear of the Lord, and find the knowledge of God.*

*6 For the Lord giveth wisdom: out of his mouth cometh knowledge and understanding.*

*7 He layeth up sound wisdom for the righteous: he is a buckler to them that walk uprightly.*

*8 He keepeth the paths of judgment, and preserveth the way of his saints.*

*9 Then shalt thou understand righteousness, and judgment, and equity; yea, every good path.*

*10 When wisdom entereth into thine heart, and knowledge is pleasant unto thy soul;*

*11 Discretion shall preserve thee, understanding shall keep thee:*

*12 To deliver thee from the way of the evil man, from the man that speaketh froward things;*

*13 Who leave the paths of uprightness, to walk in the ways of darkness;*

*14 Who rejoice to do evil, and delight in the frowardness of the wicked;*

*15 Whose ways are crooked, and they froward in their paths:*

*16 To deliver thee from the strange woman, even from the stranger which flattereth with her words;*

*17 Which forsaketh the guide of her youth, and forgetteth the covenant of her God.*

*18 For her house inclineth unto death, and her paths unto the dead.*

*19 None that go unto her return again, neither take they hold of the paths of life.*

*20 That thou mayest walk in the way of good men, and keep the paths of the righteous.*

*21 For the upright shall dwell in the land, and the perfect shall remain in it.*

*22 But the wicked shall be cut off from the earth, and the transgressors shall be rooted out of it.*

<!-- post 17148 · 2026-02-04 06:04 · study -->

**Proverbs 2** continues the instruction on Wisdom, and The importance of accepting her as the truth of God. That Spirit of truth that we as Christians are given, so long as we receive it, walking in the Spirit, walking in God's word. 

We go to church to hear the word preached (faith cometh by hearing), as we incline our ear unto wisdom. Not being, as many are described in scripture, dull of hearing. We should rather be ready to hear and to keep it in our hearts, as doers of the word. 

We should seek the wisdom of God as it were silver, a treasure. More valuable than riches is the wisdom of God's commandments. 

**Matthew 7:24** *Therefore whosoever heareth these sayings of mine, and doeth them, I will liken him unto a wise man, which built his house upon a rock*

A wise man cries for knowledge, and lifts up their voice for understanding. Asking God for His wisdom, like Solomon did. Ask and it shall be given you. Seek it and ye shall find. And with knowledge, understanding, and in doing what God says, we grow in faith.

**Hebrews 11:6** *But without faith it is impossible to please him: for he that cometh to God must believe that he is, and that he is a rewarder of them that diligently seek him.* 

*The Lord giveth wisdom.* Out of His mouth: 

**Matthew 4:4** *But he answered and said, It is written, Man shall not live by bread alone, but by every word that proceedeth out of the mouth of God.*

If wisdom proceeds out of the mouth of God, and we live by every word of God, and all scripture is given, inspired by God, written by holy men of God who spake as they were moved by the Holy Ghost, we need to have a Bible that conveys all of them. That we may be perfect, complete and throughly furnished having everything we need. In English, this is found only in the authorized version of the King James Bible, the perfect and preserved word of God.

<!-- post 17150 · 2026-02-05 05:46 · study -->

Continuing **Proverbs 2** we see the the Lord giveth wisdom, and the paths of judgment preserves the way of His saints. The paths analogy is used throughout Proverbs, and the way, is the doctrines of Christ. Early Christians were said to be followers of the way. We hear about keeping the old paths. God's word is a light unto out feet, and He directs our paths when we acknowledge Him. 

This understanding is very much the beginning of wisdom. Knowing where you've been and where you're going, seeing down the road. Knowing there's an end. Knowing the journey can lead you from the straight path, to the left or right. It is this understanding that gives us our first discernment, to see "If I continue this way, danger" knowing there are consequences "down the road." 

We see God's ways are not our own, but in His knowledge we get righteous judgement, to see every good path. The way. 

And that discretion preserves us, keeping us safe. Delivering us from the evil man, the froward who walk in paths of darkness. Or laying in wait on uncertain, or crooked ways. 

This discretion delivers us from the strange woman, the counterpoint of Wisdom. The strange woman forsakes the good paths, the ways of her youth, the covenant of God. 

Her paths lead to death. Wisdom's, to life. 

We are called to learn God's ways to avoid that snare. To walk the way of good men. To keep the paths or righteousness. And the promise of God is that, by keeping to Wisdom and her paths, we will be upright and true. His saints, sanctified in His Son. That we will dwell in the land. 

But the curse of those who don't keep the way, who choose the strange woman, and her paths of death; being cut off from the earth, and rooted our of it. 

**Jude 1:12** *These are spots in your feasts of charity, when they feast with you, feeding themselves without fear: clouds they are without water, carried about of winds; trees whose fruit withereth, without fruit, twice dead, plucked up by the roots*...

Cast into the blackness of darkness forever.

## Proverbs 3  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-06

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17152

<!-- post 17152 · 2026-02-06 05:20 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 3**
*My son, forget not my law; but let thine heart keep my commandments:*

*2 For length of days, and long life, and peace, shall they add to thee.*

*3 Let not mercy and truth forsake thee: bind them about thy neck; write them upon the table of thine heart:*

*4 So shalt thou find favour and good understanding in the sight of God and man.*

*5 Trust in the Lord with all thine heart; and lean not unto thine own understanding.*

*6 In all thy ways acknowledge him, and he shall direct thy paths.*

*7 Be not wise in thine own eyes: fear the Lord, and depart from evil.*

*8 It shall be health to thy navel, and marrow to thy bones.*

*9 Honour the Lord with thy substance, and with the firstfruits of all thine increase:*

*10 So shall thy barns be filled with plenty, and thy presses shall burst out with new wine.*

*11 My son, despise not the chastening of the Lord; neither be weary of his correction:*

*12 For whom the Lord loveth he correcteth; even as a father the son in whom he delighteth.*

*13 Happy is the man that findeth wisdom, and the man that getteth understanding.*

*14 For the merchandise of it is better than the merchandise of silver, and the gain thereof than fine gold.*

*15 She is more precious than rubies: and all the things thou canst desire are not to be compared unto her.*

*16 Length of days is in her right hand; and in her left hand riches and honour.*

*17 Her ways are ways of pleasantness, and all her paths are peace.*

*18 She is a tree of life to them that lay hold upon her: and happy is every one that retaineth her.*

*19 The Lord by wisdom hath founded the earth; by understanding hath he established the heavens.*

*20 By his knowledge the depths are broken up, and the clouds drop down the dew.*

*21 My son, let not them depart from thine eyes: keep sound wisdom and discretion:*

*22 So shall they be life unto thy soul, and grace to thy neck.*

*23 Then shalt thou walk in thy way safely, and thy foot shall not stumble.*

*24 When thou liest down, thou shalt not be afraid: yea, thou shalt lie down, and thy sleep shall be sweet.*

*25 Be not afraid of sudden fear, neither of the desolation of the wicked, when it cometh.*

*26 For the Lord shall be thy confidence, and shall keep thy foot from being taken.*

*27 Withhold not good from them to whom it is due, when it is in the power of thine hand to do it.*

*28 Say not unto thy neighbour, Go, and come again, and to morrow I will give; when thou hast it by thee.*

*29 Devise not evil against thy neighbour, seeing he dwelleth securely by thee.*

*30 Strive not with a man without cause, if he have done thee no harm.*

*31 Envy thou not the oppressor, and choose none of his ways.*

*32 For the froward is abomination to the Lord: but his secret is with the righteous.*

*33 The curse of the Lord is in the house of the wicked: but he blesseth the habitation of the just.*

*34 Surely he scorneth the scorners: but he giveth grace unto the lowly.*

*35 The wise shall inherit glory: but shame shall be the promotion of fools.*

<!-- post 17153 · 2026-02-06 05:34 · study -->

Continuing in **Proverbs 3**, building on the understanding given already; that fear of God brings knowledge, His word brings discernment, and the Wisdom of God is the fruit of following His ways over our own. 

Following the way, walking His paths, being doers of the word and not hearers only, is Wisdom. But following other paths is like going in unto a strange woman, whose ways lead to death. 

But what are the advantages of knowing and keeping God's ways? Obeying His commandments? 

*For length of days, and long life, and peace, they shall add to thee*. 

God's commandments have promises. And it just makes sense that since the wages of sin is death, righteousness is life. 

Mercy and truth are the most precious of God's commandments. His mercy towards us shows the value of mercy to others. Truth guides us to judge righteous judgment. 

Jews today literally bind the words of the law to their arms, and in boxes on their forehead. They take the promise of finding favor of God and men for "binding" literally, and *omitted the weightier matters of the law, judgment, mercy, and faith*.

*Trust in the Lord with all thine heart; and lean not unto thine own understanding. In all thy ways acknowledge him, and he shall direct thy paths.*

This is the key verse of Proverbs. The foundational understanding. Remember it, sing it, say it. And in everything acknowledge God first. 

Be not wise in your own eyes, don't lean on your understanding. Don't think to blaze your own trails and beat down your own paths. There is nothing new under the Sun and God's ways are the best ways. The old paths, tried and true. 

And following them brings health. 

And honoring the Lord by giving of your substance, your riches, the first portion of your increase, will bring prosperity. 

Note this isn't a prosperity gospel, but show that gratitude for the blessings He provides, and acknowledging Him first, will see our blessing compound. He rewards gratitude and hates murmuring.

<!-- post 17154 · 2026-02-06 05:44 · study -->

*My son, despise not the chastening of the Lord; neither be weary of his correction: For whom the Lord loveth he correcteth; even as a father the son in whom he delighteth.*

Gaining wisdom and understanding comes also from His chastening when we don't keep to His paths, when we stray to our own understanding. Like a mule that may wander off the path is whipped, so too are we chastened. And we should be thankful. 

**Hebrews 12:7-8** *ye endure chastening, God dealeth with you as with sons; for what son is he whom the father chasteneth not? But if ye be without chastisement, whereof all are partakers, then are ye bastards, and not sons.*

Be happy in chastening. Delight in His law. Gain understanding, covet Wisdom the way men covet riches, because it is more precious than silver, gold or rubies or any of the merchandise of the world. 

Wealth cannot extend your life and give you health, not like the Lord can. And love of money is the root of all evil, and for covetousness sake come wars. But pursuing Wisdom brings life. 

*Length of days is in her right hand; and in her left hand riches and honour.*

Her paths lead to peace. Wisdom is a tree of life! So by faith we step out on the paths, and keep the commandments, understanding the promises of God.

The Lord by wisdom hath founded the earth. Creation is by His knowledge and understanding. And knowing all things were created by His Word, through Christ Jesus, we see that Jesus IS the way, the truth, the light that is described throughout Proverbs. He is Wisdom. He is the tree of life. We are followers of the Way. We follow Jesus.

<!-- post 17155 · 2026-02-06 05:47 · study -->

And so we keep His word, we study His ways, we keep wisdom and discretion, with the promo that He gives us life to our soul and lays grace upon our necks. Safety in our walk, and sure steps along His paths. 

Though we walk through the valley of the shadow of death, we have no fear. We lay down to sleep, and sleep the sweet sleep of those resting under the Good Shepherd. In the arms of Jesus. 

The Lord is our confidence.

<!-- post 17156 · 2026-02-06 06:02 · study -->

Verse 27-32 can be summed up; Love thy neighbor. Give to him his due, good when it is in your power, loan to him freely when you can. Don't devise evil against him. Our neighbors dwell against us, locally. 

Jesus answers the question, "Who is our neighbor?" with the parable of the good Samaritan, leaning on this very wisdom. Our neighbors are those in our proximity who need help, and it is in our power to provide it. Love thy neighbor. 

Strive not with a man without cause. In the NT, Paul writes;

**Romans 12:8** *If it be possible, as much as lieth in you, live peaceably with all men.*

Love our enemies, certainly, but don't go out of your way to make one. Especially if they've done no harm to you. Don't be like the froward and oppressors, they are an abomination to the Lord. 

**FRO'WARD**, a. L. versus: *turned or looking from.*

- Perverse, that is, turning from, with aversion or reluctance; not willing to yield or comply with what is required; unyielding; ungovernable; refractory; disobedient; peevish; as a froward child.

Implacable, unmerciful, disobedient. It is as much an abomination to unreasonable and unyielding as being a sodomite, to the Lord. 

The Lord's curse will be on the house of the wicked. Vengeance is His. But the Lord blesses the household of the just. Those who work righteous judgement and show mercy. Peacemakers. The meek and lowly. 

This is wisdom. And as the meek inherit the earth and peacemakers are blessed, the wise shall inherit glory. 

While fools inherit shame.

## Proverbs 4  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-07

<!-- post 17157 · 2026-02-07 07:56 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 4**
*Hear, ye children, the instruction of a father, and attend to know understanding.*

*2 For I give you good doctrine, forsake ye not my law.*

*3 For I was my father's son, tender and only beloved in the sight of my mother.*

*4 He taught me also, and said unto me, Let thine heart retain my words: keep my commandments, and live.*

*5 Get wisdom, get understanding: forget it not; neither decline from the words of my mouth.*

*6 Forsake her not, and she shall preserve thee: love her, and she shall keep thee.*

*7 Wisdom is the principal thing; therefore get wisdom: and with all thy getting get understanding.*

*8 Exalt her, and she shall promote thee: she shall bring thee to honour, when thou dost embrace her.*

*9 She shall give to thine head an ornament of grace: a crown of glory shall she deliver to thee.*

*10 Hear, O my son, and receive my sayings; and the years of thy life shall be many.*

*11 I have taught thee in the way of wisdom; I have led thee in right paths.*

*12 When thou goest, thy steps shall not be straitened; and when thou runnest, thou shalt not stumble.*

*13 Take fast hold of instruction; let her not go: keep her; for she is thy life.*

*14 Enter not into the path of the wicked, and go not in the way of evil men.*

*15 Avoid it, pass not by it, turn from it, and pass away.*

*16 For they sleep not, except they have done mischief; and their sleep is taken away, unless they cause some to fall.*

*17 For they eat the bread of wickedness, and drink the wine of violence.*

*18 But the path of the just is as the shining light, that shineth more and more unto the perfect day.*

*19 The way of the wicked is as darkness: they know not at what they stumble.*

*20 My son, attend to my words; incline thine ear unto my sayings.*

*21 Let them not depart from thine eyes; keep them in the midst of thine heart.*

*22 For they are life unto those that find them, and health to all their flesh.*

*23 Keep thy heart with all diligence; for out of it are the issues of life.*

*24 Put away from thee a froward mouth, and perverse lips put far from thee.*

*25 Let thine eyes look right on, and let thine eyelids look straight before thee.*

*26 Ponder the path of thy feet, and let all thy ways be established.*

*27 Turn not to the right hand nor to the left: remove thy foot from evil.*

<!-- post 17158 · 2026-02-07 08:23 · study -->

Solomon continues giving the reader what is essentially a preface for the Book of Proverbs, that runs through chapter 9. The understanding of Wisdom vs folly, the good woman to be sought vs the strange woman, who leads to ruin. 

While there are plenty of wise sayings given in the first 9 chapters, including here in **Proverbs 4**, the thrust of Solomon's instruction is to emphasize this choice, and to encourage the pursuit of God's will, His paths, knowledge, discernment and Wisdom. 

He recalls his father David's instruction, stressing this idea. 

*Keep my commandments, and live. Get wisdom, get understanding: forget it not; neither decline from the words of my mouth. Forsake her not, and she shall preserve thee: love her, and she shall keep thee. Wisdom is the principal thing*...

She brings honor, promoting the wise, and more importantly, leads to a crown of glory through the ornament of grace. 

The pursuit of Godly wisdom is inextricably linked to accepting the truth of God in His gospel. The fear of God is the beginning of wisdom, but the end of it is grace and glory. 

Note the urgency of David in instructing his son, reflecting in Solomon's own words to his sons, and by the Spirit, to we who are sons of God. 

This should be every parent's priority, too.  Imparting this same understanding, that in wisdom is life, and that if you prioritize knowledge of God, you will not stumble, He will direct your paths, you have life in Him. 

Coupled with the warning to not follow after the wicked, the froward, the evil. Those who are only satisfied by mischief and by causing others to stumble. Taking pleasure in harming others. In violence. In offense. 

This is literally true, referring in previous chapters to those who rush to spoil and bloodshed. But it is also true of those who, rather than instruct children to go down the paths of Wisdom, instead mislead and bring children to offense. 

**Luke 17:2** *It were better for him that a millstone were hanged about his neck, and he cast into the sea, than that he should offend one of these little ones.*

And so, like David to Solomon, the king urges his children to not let these sayings depart from them. To keep them. To not turn to the left or the right. To keep our heart with all diligence. To ponder the path of our feet. In all our ways acknowledging God, that He may direct us where to go. To keep on the Way. To remove our foot from evil.

## Proverbs 5  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-08

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17165

<!-- post 17165 · 2026-02-08 07:30 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 5**
*My son, attend unto my wisdom, and bow thine ear to my understanding:*

*2 That thou mayest regard discretion, and that thy lips may keep knowledge.*

*3 For the lips of a strange woman drop as an honeycomb, and her mouth is smoother than oil:*

*4 But her end is bitter as wormwood, sharp as a two-edged sword.*

*5 Her feet go down to death; her steps take hold on hell.*

*6 Lest thou shouldest ponder the path of life, her ways are moveable, that thou canst not know them.*

*7 Hear me now therefore, O ye children, and depart not from the words of my mouth.*

*8 Remove thy way far from her, and come not nigh the door of her house:*

*9 Lest thou give thine honour unto others, and thy years unto the cruel:*

*10 Lest strangers be filled with thy wealth; and thy labours be in the house of a stranger;*

*11 And thou mourn at the last, when thy flesh and thy body are consumed,*

*12 And say, How have I hated instruction, and my heart despised reproof;*

*13 And have not obeyed the voice of my teachers, nor inclined mine ear to them that instructed me!*

*14 I was almost in all evil in the midst of the congregation and assembly.*

*15 Drink waters out of thine own cistern, and running waters out of thine own well.*

*16 Let thy fountains be dispersed abroad, and rivers of waters in the streets.*

*17 Let them be only thine own, and not strangers' with thee.*

*18 Let thy fountain be blessed: and rejoice with the wife of thy youth.*

*19 Let her be as the loving hind and pleasant roe; let her breasts satisfy thee at all times; and be thou ravished always with her love.*

*20 And why wilt thou, my son, be ravished with a strange woman, and embrace the bosom of a stranger?*

*21 For the ways of man are before the eyes of the Lord, and he pondereth all his goings.*

*22 His own iniquities shall take the wicked himself, and he shall be holden with the cords of his sins.*

*23 He shall die without instruction; and in the greatness of his folly he shall go astray.*

<!-- post 17166 · 2026-02-08 08:02 · study -->

**Proverbs 5** continues the introduction section of the book of wisdom, and the near pleading of the father, Solomon, to his son, but in the Spirit, God the Father pleading with His children, to pay attention and listen to what He is teaching us. 

His instruction on chasing Wisdom, as knowledge and discretion, and warning of chasing after the strange woman of folly. 

But Solomon being the pen used by the Penman, the analogy given in this chapter of adultery serves as both a metaphor and practical application of chasing after women who aren't yours. Something the king with a thousand wives and concubines knows all about. 

So we can apply both literal and figurative and receive wisdom from both. 

The strange woman speaks sweet sayings, and is seductive in her ways, but her end is bitter and sharp. Deadly. 

God's paths are straight and knowable, a path of life, but her ways are moveable, ultimately unknowable. The Way is laid out and the light of that path is His word. The path of death shifts and moves and is full of snares and stumbling stones. Where you could think you know where you are and what lies ahead, and suddenly you're turned around and lost. 

In an adulterous relationship, this is also true. What may start in lust and be seen as temporary, often leads somewhere else, and far away. 

As they say: Sin will take you further than you want to go, keep you longer than you want to stay, and cost you more than you want to pay. 

Don't go looking for the strange woman. Don't seek out the folly of other men's understanding. It can cost you your honor, and take away years of your life to the service of the cruel, your wealth and labors to the stranger. 

Again, literally in the case of chasing after strange women, foreign women. Unsaved women. Women who are not your wife. 

In **1 Kings 11** we read, *king Solomon loved many strange women...he had seven hundred wives, princesses, and three hundred concubines: and his wives turned away his heart. For it came to pass, when Solomon was old, that his wives turned away his heart after other gods: and his heart was not perfect with the Lord his God, as was the heart of David his father.*

The wisest man in history, who wrote these warnings to his sons and to us, fell prey to the very thing he counseled against. 

Solomon may have written this in repentance and regret. He may have been in the midst of it. It could be before he succumbed to the temptation. But it is often true that the preacher preaches what he needs to hear, more than the audience. 

Verse 11 ties the literal back to the spiritual, and death and hell that awaits those who didn't fear God and seek after His Wisdom. The regret the fool will have as his flesh and body are consumed in never ending torment. 

*How have I hated instruction, and my heart despised reproof; And have not obeyed the voice of my teachers, nor inclined mine ear to them that instructed me!* 

The shame in the midst of the assembly of being almost in all evil, when the books are opened in judgment...If physical death from a lifetime of bad choices and sin is heartbreaking, the regret in Hell for those that didn't harken to the gospel must be overwhelming. 

Solomon then compares fidelity to the wife of your youth, your first love, as with Lady Wisdom, to a fountain, to a well. To the cistern you dug. The waters are yours and you need not look for another source. 

*Let thy fountain be blessed: and rejoice with the wife of thy youth.*

Don't stray from your wife. Don't stray from the instruction of your fathers. Be ravished always with her love. Be satisfied in her. 

Our sins are before the Lord, He knows all our ways. A man's iniquities are the bonds he is caught in, the cords he is held with. Going after folly and rejecting instruction leads to a man's own sin binding him unto death.

## Proverbs 6  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-09

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17167

<!-- post 17167 · 2026-02-09 05:44 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 6**
*My son, if thou be surety for thy friend, if thou hast stricken thy hand with a stranger,*

*2 Thou art snared with the words of thy mouth, thou art taken with the words of thy mouth.*

*3 Do this now, my son, and deliver thyself, when thou art come into the hand of thy friend; go, humble thyself, and make sure thy friend.*

*4 Give not sleep to thine eyes, nor slumber to thine eyelids.*

*5 Deliver thyself as a roe from the hand of the hunter, and as a bird from the hand of the fowler.*

*6 Go to the ant, thou sluggard; consider her ways, and be wise:*

*7 Which having no guide, overseer, or ruler,*

*8 Provideth her meat in the summer, and gathereth her food in the harvest.*

*9 How long wilt thou sleep, O sluggard? when wilt thou arise out of thy sleep?*

*10 Yet a little sleep, a little slumber, a little folding of the hands to sleep:*

*11 So shall thy poverty come as one that travelleth, and thy want as an armed man.*

*12 A naughty person, a wicked man, walketh with a froward mouth.*

*13 He winketh with his eyes, he speaketh with his feet, he teacheth with his fingers;*

*14 Frowardness is in his heart, he deviseth mischief continually; he soweth discord.*

*15 Therefore shall his calamity come suddenly; suddenly shall he be broken without remedy.*

*16 These six things doth the Lord hate: yea, seven are an abomination unto him:*

*17 A proud look, a lying tongue, and hands that shed innocent blood,*

*18 An heart that deviseth wicked imaginations, feet that be swift in running to mischief,*

*19 A false witness that speaketh lies, and he that soweth discord among brethren.*

*20 My son, keep thy father's commandment, and forsake not the law of thy mother:*

*21 Bind them continually upon thine heart, and tie them about thy neck.*

*22 When thou goest, it shall lead thee; when thou sleepest, it shall keep thee; and when thou awakest, it shall talk with thee.*

*23 For the commandment is a lamp; and the law is light; and reproofs of instruction are the way of life:*

*24 To keep thee from the evil woman, from the flattery of the tongue of a strange woman.*

*25 Lust not after her beauty in thine heart; neither let her take thee with her eyelids.*

*26 For by means of a whorish woman a man is brought to a piece of bread: and the adultress will hunt for the precious life.*

*27 Can a man take fire in his bosom, and his clothes not be burned?*

*28 Can one go upon hot coals, and his feet not be burned?*

*29 So he that goeth in to his neighbour's wife; whosoever toucheth her shall not be innocent.*

*30 Men do not despise a thief, if he steal to satisfy his soul when he is hungry;*

*31 But if he be found, he shall restore sevenfold; he shall give all the substance of his house.*

*32 But whoso committeth adultery with a woman lacketh understanding: he that doeth it destroyeth his own soul.*

*33 A wound and dishonour shall he get; and his reproach shall not be wiped away.*

*34 For jealousy is the rage of a man: therefore he will not spare in the day of vengeance.*

*35 He will not regard any ransom; neither will he rest content, though thou givest many gifts.*

<!-- post 17168 · 2026-02-09 06:01 · study -->

Here in **Proverbs 6**, the instructions to his son continues, as we begin the meat of the advice Solomon, in the Spirit, gives. The wise sayings and understanding that come from the Lord though the king. Starting with financial advice.

If you act as surety for a friend, pay it. If you make a deal and agree to it, make good on it, but if you can, don't act as another man's guarantee on a loan, and if you have, don't even sleep until you can humble yourself to get out of the trap, and deliver yourself. 

Don't be lazy. Don't be a sluggard. Consider the enterprising ant. Napping and laziness leads to poverty and want. 

Stay from the naughty, the froward. They wink, they are shifty and steal. They are constantly plotting mischief and love to get people wound up and set against one another. Don't be like them. Their destruction is sure. 

The 7 things God hates; proud looks, lying, hands that shed innocent blood, hearts that devise wickedness, feet swift to mischief, a false witness and sowing discord among the brethren. This is what a naughty or froward man is. These are traits of being froward. 

Take heed not to be proud, not to lie and bare false witness against others, plotting evil schemes to sow discord, to commit violence, to do these things, especially for your own profit is to be an abomination before the Lord. 

Many do it to just take pleasure in the chaos they create.

<!-- post 17169 · 2026-02-10 05:31 · study -->

*My son, keep thy father's commandment, and forsake not the law of thy mother: Bind them continually upon thine heart, and tie them about thy neck.*

This appeal to listen to the good and Godly word, given by your parents, the instruction in righteousness, and to keep them in your heart, and allegories like it are found frequently in **Proverbs**. It is a source cited by Jews to defend their practice of "phylacteries."

**Matthew 23:4-5**
*For they bind heavy burdens and grievous to be borne, and lay them on men's shoulders; but they themselves will not move them with one of their fingers. But all their works they do for to be seen of men: they make broad their phylacteries, and enlarge the borders of their garments*,

Pharisees and scribes exaggerated religious symbols, specifically phylacteries (small leather boxes with scripture inside them, worn on the forehead and straps with scripture worn on their shoulders and wrapped in their arm, called tefillin) and garment tassels (tzitzit) to appear more pious and draw attention to themselves, rather than truly living God's law. They made the command literal, rather than in the spirit of how it was given. They lose sight of the words and living them, not being doers of the law. 

Being led by the law, on God's paths, going His way, is the meaning behind it. That when you sleep, when you wake, His word talks with you.

This is so true. When we wake in the night, if we walk in the Spirit and stay in the word, our idle thoughts become more focused on Him, and prayer and pondering His word. When we don't it is easy to have the cares of this world and fear and sin knocking at the door. 

The commandment is a lamp, a light to your feet. Reproofs of instruction are the way of life. The way, the path, the road God wants us to walk on, that we may live.

<!-- post 17170 · 2026-02-10 05:44 · study -->

Solomon again applies this wisdom and the strait path of God's way, to being tempted by strange women. Still an analogy of foolishness, but also the very real danger of sin. 

Keep God's word, the law of your mother handed down to you, near in your thoughts and heart, to keep thee from the evil woman, her flattering tongue, beguiling the young man. *Lust not after her beauty in thine heart...*

**Matthew 5:27-28**
*Ye have heard that it was said by them of old time, Thou shalt not commit adultery: But I say unto you, That whosoever looketh on a woman to lust after her hath committed adultery with her already in his heart.*

Jesus' words are the words of God. He is not doing away with the law, He isn't really adding to it, but clarifying the word and wisdom He gave to Solomon by the Holy Spirit. Giving further understanding. 

Because, the way of the harlot is tempting. Her lips and eyes, a trap. Men are ruined by these lusts. The adulteress hunts for the precious life, to take wealth and honor from the foolish man. 

Today, how many married men are taken in by pornography, Only Fans whores, and internet cat fishing schemes? 

Lust of the eyes is a powerful thing.  *Can a man take fire in his bosom, and his clothes not be burned?*

The horrible sin of coveting your neighbors wife, going in to her, laying with her, it's an evil thing before God. And it is so easily avoided. Drink from your own cistern. Delight in the wife of thy youth. Don't look upon the strange woman with lust. 

God says that a thief is less condemnable because he can pay back what he stole. An adulterer can only give his life to be satisfied by the jealous husband.

## Proverbs 7  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-11

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17171

<!-- post 17171 · 2026-02-11 05:44 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 7**
*My son, keep my words, and lay up my commandments with thee.*

*2 Keep my commandments, and live; and my law as the apple of thine eye.*

*3 Bind them upon thy fingers, write them upon the table of thine heart.*

*4 Say unto wisdom, Thou art my sister; and call understanding thy kinswoman:*

*5 That they may keep thee from the strange woman, from the stranger which flattereth with her words.*

*6 For at the window of my house I looked through my casement,*

*7 And beheld among the simple ones, I discerned among the youths, a young man void of understanding,*

*8 Passing through the street near her corner; and he went the way to her house,*

*9 In the twilight, in the evening, in the black and dark night:*

*10 And, behold, there met him a woman with the attire of an harlot, and subtil of heart.*

*11 (She is loud and stubborn; her feet abide not in her house:*

*12 Now is she without, now in the streets, and lieth in wait at every corner.)*

*13 So she caught him, and kissed him, and with an impudent face said unto him,*

*14 I have peace offerings with me; this day have I payed my vows.*

*15 Therefore came I forth to meet thee, diligently to seek thy face, and I have found thee.*

*16 I have decked my bed with coverings of tapestry, with carved works, with fine linen of Egypt.*

*17 I have perfumed my bed with myrrh, aloes, and cinnamon.*

*18 Come, let us take our fill of love until the morning: let us solace ourselves with loves.*

*19 For the goodman is not at home, he is gone a long journey:*

*20 He hath taken a bag of money with him, and will come home at the day appointed.*

*21 With her much fair speech she caused him to yield, with the flattering of her lips she forced him.*

*22 He goeth after her straightway, as an ox goeth to the slaughter, or as a fool to the correction of the stocks;*

*23 Till a dart strike through his liver; as a bird hasteth to the snare, and knoweth not that it is for his life.*

*24 Hearken unto me now therefore, O ye children, and attend to the words of my mouth.*

*25 Let not thine heart decline to her ways, go not astray in her paths.*

*26 For she hath cast down many wounded: yea, many strong men have been slain by her.*

*27 Her house is the way to hell, going down to the chambers of death.*

<!-- post 17172 · 2026-02-11 06:04 · study -->

Again, Solomon appeals to his son to take heed to wisdom, to bind it in his heart. To keep the commandments he's given him that he may live. The law as the apple of his eye. Making wisdom and understanding your sister and kinswoman that they keep you from the strange woman, which is death. 

To illustrate, he gives a parable of a young man, void of understanding, who encounters a woman dressed as a harlot, who subtly entices him to bed. 

He says such a woman is loud and stubborn and exists on every corner, just as folly and temptations are around every bend in the way.

She catches him, kisses him and saying she's holy, having made her offerings and confirming her vows, she beguiles him. Sheep's clothing for a ravenous wolf. 

She appeals with flattery, she sought him special, seeking to find only him, while she is a snare to any who cross her path. 

She appeals with the lust of the flesh. With enticing sensual carnality. Perfumed bed, preciously adorned to spend the night in passion. To take the fire to his  bosom. To spark that desire in his heart. 

She says the Goodman of the house is not home, and gone for a long journey...making it illicit and adultery. There likely is no "goodman" as a harlot keeps no husband. But there is an allegory here for sin and the temptation that judgment of God is afar off. The Goodman which is Christ, not here. 

Similar to the warning of complacency Jesus gives us;

**Matthew 24:44-51**
*Therefore be ye also ready: for in such an hour as ye think not the Son of man cometh. Who then is a faithful and wise servant, whom his lord hath made ruler over his household, to give them meat in due season? Blessed is that servant, whom his lord when he cometh shall find so doing. Verily I say unto you, That he shall make him ruler over all his goods. But and if that evil servant shall say in his heart, My lord delayeth his coming; And shall begin to smite his fellowservants, and to eat and drink with the drunken; The lord of that servant shall come in a day when he looketh not for him, and in an hour that he is not aware of, And shall cut him asunder, and appoint him his portion with the hypocrites: there shall be weeping and gnashing of teeth.*

There is an allure to this idea, that we may justify sin by the distance of judgment. By the illusion of safety given by distance and time. But all who are fooled by this are found out. 

And so; *With her much fair speech she caused him to yield, with the flattering of her lips she forced him.*

Temptations often come through flattery and fair speech, the things we make rationalizations with, like weaving a net for a snare. 

And the fool falls headlong into it. Until they get trapped. The dart in the liver. The knife taking their life. Like a bird in a net. 

This parable, taken literally, has played out innumerable times. We may all know simple young men who have fallen for it. But allegorically, it is true of folly, of rejecting wisdom for sin. For rejecting God's path of life to go our own way. 

*For she hath cast down many wounded: yea, many strong men have been slain by her. Her house is the way to hell, going down to the chambers of death.*

## Proverbs 8  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-12

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17173

<!-- post 17173 · 2026-02-12 05:46 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 8**
*Doth not wisdom cry? and understanding put forth her voice?*

*2 She standeth in the top of high places, by the way in the places of the paths.*

*3 She crieth at the gates, at the entry of the city, at the coming in at the doors.*

*4 Unto you, O men, I call; and my voice is to the sons of man.*

*5 O ye simple, understand wisdom: and, ye fools, be ye of an understanding heart.*

*6 Hear; for I will speak of excellent things; and the opening of my lips shall be right things.*

*7 For my mouth shall speak truth; and wickedness is an abomination to my lips.*

*8 All the words of my mouth are in righteousness; there is nothing froward or perverse in them.*

*9 They are all plain to him that understandeth, and right to them that find knowledge.*

*10 Receive my instruction, and not silver; and knowledge rather than choice gold.*

*11 For wisdom is better than rubies; and all the things that may be desired are not to be compared to it.*

*12 I wisdom dwell with prudence, and find out knowledge of witty inventions.*

*13 The fear of the Lord is to hate evil: pride, and arrogancy, and the evil way, and the froward mouth, do I hate.*

*14 Counsel is mine, and sound wisdom: I am understanding; I have strength.*

*15 By me kings reign, and princes decree justice.*

*16 By me princes rule, and nobles, even all the judges of the earth.*

*17 I love them that love me; and those that seek me early shall find me.*

*18 Riches and honour are with me; yea, durable riches and righteousness.*

*19 My fruit is better than gold, yea, than fine gold; and my revenue than choice silver.*

*20 I lead in the way of righteousness, in the midst of the paths of judgment:*

*21 That I may cause those that love me to inherit substance; and I will fill their treasures.*

*22 The Lord possessed me in the beginning of his way, before his works of old.*

*23 I was set up from everlasting, from the beginning, or ever the earth was.*

*24 When there were no depths, I was brought forth; when there were no fountains abounding with water.*

*25 Before the mountains were settled, before the hills was I brought forth:*

*26 While as yet he had not made the earth, nor the fields, nor the highest part of the dust of the world.*

*27 When he prepared the heavens, I was there: when he set a compass upon the face of the depth:*

*28 When he established the clouds above: when he strengthened the fountains of the deep:*

*29 When he gave to the sea his decree, that the waters should not pass his commandment: when he appointed the foundations of the earth:*

*30 Then I was by him, as one brought up with him: and I was daily his delight, rejoicing always before him;*

*31 Rejoicing in the habitable part of his earth; and my delights were with the sons of men.*

*32 Now therefore hearken unto me, O ye children: for blessed are they that keep my ways.*

*33 Hear instruction, and be wise, and refuse it not.*

*34 Blessed is the man that heareth me, watching daily at my gates, waiting at the posts of my doors.*

*35 For whoso findeth me findeth life, and shall obtain favour of the Lord.*

*36 But he that sinneth against me wrongeth his own soul: all they that hate me love death.*

<!-- post 17174 · 2026-02-12 06:02 · study -->

**Proverbs 8 (& 9)**, are an Ode to Wisdom, the Word and Spirit of God, with Him in the beginning , through Whom all creation was made. 

Wisdom cries out from all creation, from the top of the mountains, in the high places and in the paths. The Lord's way is wisdom. In the coming and going of the city. At its gates and doors. 

Like God calls to all men, as Jesus lifted up, wisdom calls out to all. To the simple and fools. To those who love wisdom and seek her excellent things, her right things. To those that seek truth. 

**John 14:6** *Jesus saith unto him, I am the way, the truth, and the life: no man cometh unto the Father, but by me.*

**Matthew 7:7** *Ask, and it shall be given you; seek, and ye shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you*

**John 8:32** *And ye shall know the truth, and the truth shall make you free*

The words of wisdom are the word of God, and there is nothing perverse or froward in them. Holy and plain to those that listen. 

More precious than any treasures, wisdom is better than gold. With wisdom dwells prudence; Wisdom applied to practice. Discernment and action according to knowledge. 

The wise hate evil; pride and arrogance, froward mouths they hate.

By wisdom kings and princes rule. 

*I love them that love me; and those that seek me early shall find me*.

The fear of God is the beginning of wisdom. 

**Psalm 63:1** *O God, thou art my God; early will I seek thee: my soul thirsteth for thee, my flesh longeth for thee in a dry and thirsty land, where no water is* 

The Lord's wisdom is from everlasting, His word. Before all creation, He is. Eternally with Him, as one brought up together. 

**John 1:1-2** *In the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God. The same was in the beginning with God.*

Blessed is the man who finds wisdom. They receive life and favor of the Lord. They live in His grace. 

But woe to those that hate wisdom. *All they that hate me love death.*

<!-- post 17175 · 2026-02-12 06:46 · study -->

No surviving canonical list from the first three centuries includes the deuterocanonicals as part of the closed Old Testament canon. The earliest inclusions appear in 4th-century sources. 

Here is a popular poem used to teach the books of the Bible.

**Concerning the Genuine Books of Divinely Inspired Scripture**

*But let not extraneous books seduce your mind, For many malignant writings have been disseminated.*

*Accept, O friend, this my approved number.*

*The historical books are twelve in all:*
*First, Genesis; then Exodus; Leviticus next; Numbers; Deuteronomy; then Joshua the son of Nun;*
*Judges, and Ruth the Moabitess with it; The four books of the Kings; and two of Chronicles;*

*Then Esdras, first and second [*Ezra-Nehemiah*].*

*Then Job the sufferer, Psalms of David the singer, The Proverbs of Solomon, Ecclesiastes,*

*And the Song of Songs, the bridal chamber of the bride.*

*The twelve [minor] prophets as one book, then Isaiah, Jeremiah with Lamentations, Ezekiel, Daniel.*

*Now count also the books of the New Mystery:*

*Matthew indeed wrote for the Hebrews the wonderful works of Christ, Mark for Italy, Luke for Greece,*
*John, the great preacher, for all, walking in heaven.[*Gospel of John and Revelation of Christ*]*

*Then the Acts of the wise apostles,*
*And fourteen Epistles of Paul,*

*And seven catholic epistles, of which James is one,*

*Two of Peter, three of John again,*

*And the last is the work of Jude the kinsman of Christ.*

*This is the sure canon of the divinely inspired Scriptures. Anything beyond these is not genuine.*

- Gregory of Nazianzus (c. 329–390)

## Proverbs 9  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-13

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17179

<!-- post 17179 · 2026-02-13 05:34 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 9**
*Wisdom hath builded her house, she hath hewn out her seven pillars:*

*2 She hath killed her beasts; she hath mingled her wine; she hath also furnished her table.*

*3 She hath sent forth her maidens: she crieth upon the highest places of the city,*

*4 Whoso is simple, let him turn in hither: as for him that wanteth understanding, she saith to him,*

*5 Come, eat of my bread, and drink of the wine which I have mingled.*

*6 Forsake the foolish, and live; and go in the way of understanding.*

*7 He that reproveth a scorner getteth to himself shame: and he that rebuketh a wicked man getteth himself a blot.*

*8 Reprove not a scorner, lest he hate thee: rebuke a wise man, and he will love thee.*

*9 Give instruction to a wise man, and he will be yet wiser: teach a just man, and he will increase in learning.*

*10 The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom: and the knowledge of the holy is understanding.*

*11 For by me thy days shall be multiplied, and the years of thy life shall be increased.*

*12 If thou be wise, thou shalt be wise for thyself: but if thou scornest, thou alone shalt bear it.*

*13 A foolish woman is clamorous: she is simple, and knoweth nothing.*

*14 For she sitteth at the door of her house, on a seat in the high places of the city,*

*15 To call passengers who go right on their ways:*

*16 Whoso is simple, let him turn in hither: and as for him that wanteth understanding, she saith to him,*

*17 Stolen waters are sweet, and bread eaten in secret is pleasant.*

*18 But he knoweth not that the dead are there; and that her guests are in the depths of hell.*

<!-- post 17180 · 2026-02-13 05:52 · study -->

**Proverbs 9** concludes the preface of Proverbs, the instruction of Solomon to his son on Lady Wisdom and the strange woman of folly. 

Wisdom's table is set for the feast, her house ready, her wine mingled (the restoration of the preserved fruit of the vine mixed with water, spiced with myrrh, sweetened with honey), her maids sent to call all who would enter in to come. 

*Whoso is simple, let him turn in hither: as for him that wanteth understanding, she saith to him, Come, eat of my bread, and drink of the wine which I have mingled.*

The unadulterated truth of God's word is that wine. The bread which has come down from heaven is Jesus. 

**John 6:32-36**
*Then Jesus said unto them, Verily, verily, I say unto you, Moses gave you not that bread from heaven; but my Father giveth you the true bread from heaven. For the bread of God is he which cometh down from heaven, and giveth life unto the world. Then said they unto him, Lord, evermore give us this bread. And Jesus said unto them, I am the bread of life: he that cometh to me shall never hunger; and he that believeth on me shall never thirst. But I said unto you, That ye also have seen me, and believe not.*

Her maidens calling out to the feast of Wisdom as the parable of the wedding feast:

**Luke 14:16-23**
*Then said he unto him, A certain man made a great supper, and bade many: and sent his servant at supper time to say to them that were bidden, Come; for all things are now ready. And they all with one consent began to make excuse. The first said unto him, I have bought a piece of ground, and I must needs go and see it: I pray thee have me excused. And another said, I have bought five yoke of oxen, and I go to prove them: I pray thee have me excused. And another said, I have married a wife, and therefore I cannot come. So that servant came, and shewed his lord these things. Then the master of the house being angry said to his servant, Go out quickly into the streets and lanes of the city, and bring in hither the poor, and the maimed, and the halt, and the blind. And the servant said, Lord, it is done as thou hast commanded, and yet there is room. And the lord said unto the servant, Go out into the highways and hedges, and compel them to come in, that my house may be filled*.

Just as both the simple and the wise are compelled to turn hither to Wisdom's feast, so too the gospel is for both good and bad;

So those servants went out into the highways, and gathered together all as many as they found, both bad and good: and the wedding was furnished with guests.

**Matthew 22:10**
*So those servants went out into the highways, and gathered together all as many as they found, both bad and good: and the wedding was furnished with guests.*

Jesus crying out in the streets, telling all to come to Him; 

**John 7:37-38**
*In the last day, that great day of the feast, Jesus stood and cried, saying, If any man thirst, let him come unto me, and drink. He that believeth on me, as the scripture hath said, out of his belly shall flow rivers of living water.*

Forsake the foolish and live! Whosoever will, let him come.

**Revelation 22:17**
*And the Spirit and the bride say, Come. And let him that heareth say, Come. And let him that is athirst come. And whosoever will, let him take the water of life freely*.

The invitation of God to wisdom, to knowledge and understanding, is the invitation to life; as a wedding feast laid out by the host. Where the wise enters into the meal already prepared. The wine mingled. The wedding clothes, laid out, white and pure. To eat that bread of life and drink that living water, never to hunger or thirst again.

<!-- post 17181 · 2026-02-13 06:14 · study -->

Solomon then gives this instruction; *Forsake the foolish, and live.* He says to not reprove a scorner, to not rebuke the wicked. This is echoed in the way Christ gave to His disciples when sending them out to the lost sheep of Israel;

**Mathew 10:14** *And whosoever shall not receive you, nor hear your words, when ye depart out of that house or city, shake off the dust of your feet*.

As Paul instructs Titus;

**Titus 3:10** *A man that is an heretick after the first and second admonition reject*

These men oppose themselves. 

Peter writes that we should not argue with scoffers; *Not rendering evil for evil, or railing for railing*. 

**1 Peter 3:16** *Having a good conscience; that, whereas they speak evil of you, as of evildoers, they may be ashamed that falsely accuse your good conversation in Christ.*

When we are out in the hedges and highways, compelling all to come to the wisdom of God, by His gospel, we aren't there to argue. To debate. We are not supposed to waste our time with scoffers and mockers and scorners of the truth. We are supposed to shake the dust and move on. 

But, if we give instruction to the wise, they will be wiser. Those seeking the truth will hear us. They will understand that the fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom, and the knowledge of the holy is understanding. 

And by His wisdom, our days are lengthened, our life increased. 

It is on every man to bear this. To the wise, for themselves and to life; for the scorner, to themselves unto death. 

This is individual soul liberty. We carry our own guilt and are responsible. But by faith we enter into His grace.

<!-- post 17182 · 2026-02-13 06:20 · study -->

In contrast to the feast of wisdom and the prudence of going forth to invite all, the strange woman sits at the door of her house, and calls to those who cross her path. 

She calls to the simple, that so often turn in to her. And, to the wise, she tempts them. Rather than with the sumptuous feast of wisdom, she offers the lure of covetousness. Of the appeal to the evil of the heart. Stolen waters are sweet, and secret bread pleasant. The heart that desires that which isn't theirs can guide even the wise to folly and sin. 

As the Lord said to Cain*, if thou doest not well, sin lieth at the door.* Sin becomes a master unto you. 

Those that enter in to folly, to sin, wise and fools, enter into death, for her guests are in the depths of Hell.
