<!-- post 15533 · 2025-01-09 16:03 · study -->

This is why it's important not to get into arguments with people whose entire premise is false. Who aren't arguing in good faith. They aren't looking for an honest debate they just want to justify being scornful or railing against you. 

It's one thing to rebuke or reprove and move on. But it's another to get dragged down into the mire with them. It's okay to walk away. To leave a chat or to block someone. That doesn't mean that you're admitting defeat as they would like to believe. It means you're not giving that which is holy to dogs.

## Proverbs 10  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-14

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17183

<!-- post 17183 · 2026-02-14 07:32 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 10**
*The proverbs of Solomon. A wise son maketh a glad father: but a foolish son is the heaviness of his mother.*

*2 Treasures of wickedness profit nothing: but righteousness delivereth from death.*

*3 The Lord will not suffer the soul of the righteous to famish: but he casteth away the substance of the wicked.*

*4 He becometh poor that dealeth with a slack hand: but the hand of the diligent maketh rich.*

*5 He that gathereth in summer is a wise son: but he that sleepeth in harvest is a son that causeth shame.*

*6 Blessings are upon the head of the just: but violence covereth the mouth of the wicked.*

*7 The memory of the just is blessed: but the name of the wicked shall rot.*

*8 The wise in heart will receive commandments: but a prating fool shall fall.*

*9 He that walketh uprightly walketh surely: but he that perverteth his ways shall be known.*

*10 He that winketh with the eye causeth sorrow: but a prating fool shall fall.*

*11 The mouth of a righteous man is a well of life: but violence covereth the mouth of the wicked.*

*12 Hatred stirreth up strifes: but love covereth all sins.*

*13 In the lips of him that hath understanding wisdom is found: but a rod is for the back of him that is void of understanding.*

*14 Wise men lay up knowledge: but the mouth of the foolish is near destruction.*

*15 The rich man's wealth is his strong city: the destruction of the poor is their poverty.*

*16 The labour of the righteous tendeth to life: the fruit of the wicked to sin.*

*17 He is in the way of life that keepeth instruction: but he that refuseth reproof erreth.*

*18 He that hideth hatred with lying lips, and he that uttereth a slander, is a fool.*

*19 In the multitude of words there wanteth not sin: but he that refraineth his lips is wise.*

*20 The tongue of the just is as choice silver: the heart of the wicked is little worth.*

*21 The lips of the righteous feed many: but fools die for want of wisdom.*

*22 The blessing of the Lord, it maketh rich, and he addeth no sorrow with it.*

*23 It is as sport to a fool to do mischief: but a man of understanding hath wisdom.*

*24 The fear of the wicked, it shall come upon him: but the desire of the righteous shall be granted.*

*25 As the whirlwind passeth, so is the wicked no more: but the righteous is an everlasting foundation.*

*26 As vinegar to the teeth, and as smoke to the eyes, so is the sluggard to them that send him.*

*27 The fear of the Lord prolongeth days: but the years of the wicked shall be shortened.*

*28 The hope of the righteous shall be gladness: but the expectation of the wicked shall perish.*

*29 The way of the Lord is strength to the upright: but destruction shall be to the workers of iniquity.*

*30 The righteous shall never be removed: but the wicked shall not inhabit the earth.*

*31 The mouth of the just bringeth forth wisdom: but the froward tongue shall be cut out.*

*32 The lips of the righteous know what is acceptable: but the mouth of the wicked speaketh frowardness.*

<!-- post 17184 · 2026-02-14 08:19 · study -->

**Proverbs 10** begins the more familiar listing of wise sayings, made in the Spirit by Solomon, his proverbs. But the lesson contrasting the righteous listening to instruction, being industrious, refraining from speaking evil and being wise, with the fool who is lazy, full of iniquity and violence, with a froward mouth, continues to be the primary theme carried over from the introduction in chapters 1-9. 

Verse 1 contains an eternal truth that any parent could attest to. Establishing early the wisdom to found is accurate and true. A wise son is the pride of a dad, while the foolish son is the heaviness and sorrow of his mother. 

From this base, the sayings stack up, precept upon precept, truth on truth. Building a foundation of wisdom that, sadly is lacking in the world today, because most, even many Christians, don't harken to the word of God. They do not fear Him, and so do not have His wisdom to rely on. They do not acknowledge Him in all their ways, and so He isn't there to direct their paths. 

There are also proverbs given that have become ingrained in the secular lexicon (even if they don't acknowledge it). Such as the first half of verse 2; Gain made by wicked deeds profit nothing. In other words, crime doesn't pay. But the wise counter to this truth is that the way of the righteous delivers one from death. 

We are told the truth of the Lord that He will provide for His own, the righteous won't famish, while He destroys the substance of the wicked. Not a prosperity gospel, but the understanding that when you walk in the Spirit, working in righteousness, the Lord will take care of your basic needs. 

**Luke 12:27-31**
*Consider the lilies how they grow: they toil not, they spin not; and yet I say unto you, that Solomon in all his glory was not arrayed like one of these. If then God so clothe the grass, which is to day in the field, and to morrow is cast into the oven; how much more will he clothe you, O ye of little faith? And seek not ye what ye shall eat, or what ye shall drink, neither be ye of doubtful mind. For all these things do the nations of the world seek after: and your Father knoweth that ye have need of these things. But rather seek ye the kingdom of God; and all these things shall be added unto you.*

This is a promise of Christ Jesus Himself, with the caveat that the poor often are that way by being slackers, not diligent in their works.

**2 Thessalonians 3:10**
*For even when we were with you, this we commanded you, that if any would not work, neither should he eat.*

God blesses those that prepare, gathering in the summer against the winter is wise. Sleeping through the harvest is a shame. Being forward thinking and considering the end of something, understanding consequences, cause and effect, is wisdom. Shortsighted sin leads to shame. 

Of course, the harvest analogy is not just applicable to agrarian cultures, or to earthly financial goals, but also the sharing of the gospel of Christ's Kingdom is frequently likened to the harvest. 

**John 4:35**
*Say not ye, There are yet four months, and then cometh harvest? behold, I say unto you, Lift up your eyes, and look on the fields; for they are white already to harvest.*

Go soul-winning! The workers are few and the harvest is plenty. He that winneth souls is wise. 

**Psalm 126:6**
*He that goeth forth and weepeth, bearing precious seed, shall doubtless come again with rejoicing, bringing his sheaves with him.*

But the sluggard who buries his talent of silver, to mix metaphors, he will be ashamed. He will not have sheaves of glory to rejoice in.

Being a just man, fair and merciful, extending grace to others, brings blessings and glory. The Lord will esteem the name of the just but the fool will die unknown, they will fall. 

Walk upright, consider what you say. Don't be a prattling fool or a subtle and froward man, or a man of violence and offense, stirring up strifes and hatred. Knowing love covereth all sins. 

**1 Peter 4:8**
*And above all things have fervent charity among yourselves: for charity shall cover the multitude of sins.*

<!-- post 17185 · 2026-02-14 08:19 · study -->

Wisdom is for the lips of the man with understanding, the rod is for the back of those without it. The wise lay up knowledge against the future, keeping instruction, but the fool is always near destruction and refuses to be corrected. 

The chapter is rich with promises for the righteous, including the desires of those walking the path of the Lord being granted, while the wicked do without and live in want and fear. 

**Mark 11:24**
*Therefore I say unto you, What things soever ye desire, when ye pray, believe that ye receive them, and ye shall have them.*

**Psalm 37:4-5**
*Delight thyself also in the Lord: and he shall give thee the desires of thine heart. Commit thy way unto the Lord; trust also in him; and he shall bring it to pass.*

The consistent promise of God for those that trust in Him is repeatedly given throughout the Bible. 

But seek the treasure in Heaven, not riches on this earth. Opportunity to serve God, to win souls, to help the poor and heartbroken. To judge the fatherless. Ask for this treasure and you will be blessed beyond measure. 

The fear of the Lord prolongs your days. Brings knowledge and understanding. Provides for your needs and will grant you your desires.

 A wise man watches what He says, giving wisdom and speaking truth. The mouth of the wicked is perverse. 

*The way of the Lord is strength to the upright: but destruction shall be to the workers of iniquity. The righteous shall never be removed: but the wicked shall not inhabit the earth*.

Amen

## Proverbs 10  ·  Series study (other pass)  ·  2025-01-10

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/15537

<!-- post 15537 · 2025-01-10 05:31 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 10**
*The proverbs of Solomon. A wise son maketh a glad father: but a foolish son is the heaviness of his mother.*

*2 Treasures of wickedness profit nothing: but righteousness delivereth from death.*

*3 The Lord will not suffer the soul of the righteous to famish: but he casteth away the substance of the wicked.*

*4 He becometh poor that dealeth with a slack hand: but the hand of the diligent maketh rich.*

*5 He that gathereth in summer is a wise son: but he that sleepeth in harvest is a son that causeth shame.*

*6 Blessings are upon the head of the just: but violence covereth the mouth of the wicked.*

*7 The memory of the just is blessed: but the name of the wicked shall rot.*

*8 The wise in heart will receive commandments: but a prating fool shall fall.*

*9 He that walketh uprightly walketh surely: but he that perverteth his ways shall be known.*

*10 He that winketh with the eye causeth sorrow: but a prating fool shall fall.*

*11 The mouth of a righteous man is a well of life: but violence covereth the mouth of the wicked.*

*12 Hatred stirreth up strifes: but love covereth all sins.*

*13 In the lips of him that hath understanding wisdom is found: but a rod is for the back of him that is void of understanding.*

*14 Wise men lay up knowledge: but the mouth of the foolish is near destruction.*

*15 The rich man's wealth is his strong city: the destruction of the poor is their poverty.*

*16 The labour of the righteous tendeth to life: the fruit of the wicked to sin.*

*17 He is in the way of life that keepeth instruction: but he that refuseth reproof erreth.*

*18 He that hideth hatred with lying lips, and he that uttereth a slander, is a fool.*

*19 In the multitude of words there wanteth not sin: but he that refraineth his lips is wise.*

*20 The tongue of the just is as choice silver: the heart of the wicked is little worth.*

*21 The lips of the righteous feed many: but fools die for want of wisdom.*

*22 The blessing of the Lord, it maketh rich, and he addeth no sorrow with it.*

*23 It is as sport to a fool to do mischief: but a man of understanding hath wisdom.*

*24 The fear of the wicked, it shall come upon him: but the desire of the righteous shall be granted.*

*25 As the whirlwind passeth, so is the wicked no more: but the righteous is an everlasting foundation.*

*26 As vinegar to the teeth, and as smoke to the eyes, so is the sluggard to them that send him.*

*27 The fear of the Lord prolongeth days: but the years of the wicked shall be shortened.*

*28 The hope of the righteous shall be gladness: but the expectation of the wicked shall perish.*

*29 The way of the Lord is strength to the upright: but destruction shall be to the workers of iniquity.*

*30 The righteous shall never be removed: but the wicked shall not inhabit the earth.*

*31 The mouth of the just bringeth forth wisdom: but the froward tongue shall be cut out.*

*32 The lips of the righteous know what is acceptable: but the mouth of the wicked speaketh frowardness*

## Proverbs 11  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-15

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17187

<!-- post 17187 · 2026-02-15 06:21 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 11**
*A false balance is abomination to the Lord: but a just weight is his delight.*

*2 When pride cometh, then cometh shame: but with the lowly is wisdom.*

*3 The integrity of the upright shall guide them: but the perverseness of transgressors shall destroy them.*

*4 Riches profit not in the day of wrath: but righteousness delivereth from death.*

*5 The righteousness of the perfect shall direct his way: but the wicked shall fall by his own wickedness.*

*6 The righteousness of the upright shall deliver them: but transgressors shall be taken in their own naughtiness.*

*7 When a wicked man dieth, his expectation shall perish: and the hope of unjust men perisheth.*

*8 The righteous is delivered out of trouble, and the wicked cometh in his stead.*

*9 An hypocrite with his mouth destroyeth his neighbour: but through knowledge shall the just be delivered.*

*10 When it goeth well with the righteous, the city rejoiceth: and when the wicked perish, there is shouting.*

*11 By the blessing of the upright the city is exalted: but it is overthrown by the mouth of the wicked.*

*12 He that is void of wisdom despiseth his neighbour: but a man of understanding holdeth his peace.*

*13 A talebearer revealeth secrets: but he that is of a faithful spirit concealeth the matter.*

*14 Where no counsel is, the people fall: but in the multitude of counsellors there is safety.*

*15 He that is surety for a stranger shall smart for it: and he that hateth suretiship is sure.*

*16 A gracious woman retaineth honour: and strong men retain riches.*

*17 The merciful man doeth good to his own soul: but he that is cruel troubleth his own flesh.*

*18 The wicked worketh a deceitful work: but to him that soweth righteousness shall be a sure reward.*

*19 As righteousness tendeth to life: so he that pursueth evil pursueth it to his own death.*

*20 They that are of a froward heart are abomination to the Lord: but such as are upright in their way are his delight.*

*21 Though hand join in hand, the wicked shall not be unpunished: but the seed of the righteous shall be delivered.*

*22 As a jewel of gold in a swine's snout, so is a fair woman which is without discretion.*

*23 The desire of the righteous is only good: but the expectation of the wicked is wrath.*

*24 There is that scattereth, and yet increaseth; and there is that withholdeth more than is meet, but it tendeth to poverty.*

*25 The liberal soul shall be made fat: and he that watereth shall be watered also himself.*

*26 He that withholdeth corn, the people shall curse him: but blessing shall be upon the head of him that selleth it.*

*27 He that diligently seeketh good procureth favour: but he that seeketh mischief, it shall come unto him.*

*28 He that trusteth in his riches shall fall; but the righteous shall flourish as a branch.*

*29 He that troubleth his own house shall inherit the wind: and the fool shall be servant to the wise of heart.*

*30 The fruit of the righteous is a tree of life; and he that winneth souls is wise.*

*31 Behold, the righteous shall be recompensed in the earth: much more the wicked and the sinner.*

<!-- post 17188 · 2026-02-15 06:56 · study -->

The main themes presented in chapter 11 see the ways of the righteous compared to the transgressor, in matters of finance, fortune and benefit, and spiritual gain. 

The delight of the Lord in our keeping of integrity and honesty in our dealings. Just measures, not cheating others, being upright in our dealings. God places an emphasis on our doing well for others when we could, in secret, selfishly gain at their expense. Doing good when no one is watching, while the froward man's perverse ways destroy them. 

He warns that pride leads to shame and humility shows wisdom. How often have we seen pride lead to an almost immediate fall? In our own lives alone! Embarrassed and ashamed when we are puffed up. But walking in humility, we receive glory from God and others. 

Solomon's wisdom shows a repeated contrast between the righteous being delivered and the wicked destroyed. That the generosity of the righteous builds up more wealth, the liberal soul made fat. Not only in this life, but the next. 

*Riches profit not in the day of wrath: but righteousness delivereth from death,* reminds us that at the judgment, all the wealth in the world will do nothing to spare the wicked from wrath. Only the righteousness that is in Christ can. We are not appointed to wrath, by His mercy and imputed righteousness. 

*The righteous is delivered out of trouble, and the wicked cometh in his stead.*

And so we should put the Kingdom first in our hearts;

Matthew 6:19-21
*Lay not up for yourselves treasures upon earth, where moth and rust doth corrupt, and where thieves break through and steal: But lay up for yourselves treasures in heaven, where neither moth nor rust doth corrupt, and where thieves do not break through nor steal: For where your treasure is, there will your heart be also.*

A warning that those that hoard and withhold being generous, or not selling their excess, will lead to poverty and worse in the day of judgement. 

God loves a cheerful giver and sees fair and just enterprise as good. But hoarding and coveting, helps no one.

**Luke 12:16-21**
*And he spake a parable unto them, saying, The ground of a certain rich man brought forth plentifully: And he thought within himself, saying, What shall I do, because I have no room where to bestow my fruits? And he said, This will I do: I will pull down my barns, and build greater; and there will I bestow all my fruits and my goods. And I will say to my soul, Soul, thou hast much goods laid up for many years; take thine ease, eat, drink, and be merry. But God said unto him, Thou fool, this night thy soul shall be required of thee: then whose shall those things be, which thou hast provided? So is he that layeth up treasure for himself, and is not rich toward God.*

In the judgment, the hope of the wicked perishes, they shall not go unpunished. Their expectation is wrath. While the just and righteous shall be delivered. 

The righteous, those who are fair, just, walking upright, speaking truth, walking in the way of the Lord, not only benefit themselves, but their community. Their city rejoices in them. They bring prosperity to their people. They are a blessing. 

The wicked, however, curse their neighbors and overthrow their city. Being void of wisdom and unable to give counsel, despising their neighbors, bring destruction. They reveal secrets and are quick to mischief, and people suffer for their evil.

<!-- post 17189 · 2026-02-15 07:06 · study -->

*He that is surety for a stranger shall smart for it: and he that hateth suretiship is sure.* 

In **Proverbs 6**, we saw Solomon warning against surety, for a friend or a stranger, and here we see a similar warning. The seeming contradiction, that one who disfavors backing another's debts is seen by God as more sure than one who gives their pledge for a stranger. That they will regret it. 

The only surety we can count on is God's. In scripture the Holy Spirit is described as the "earnest" or seal guaranteeing a believer's eternal inheritance and salvation. The surety of our eternal life. 

**Ephesians 1:12-14**
*That we should be to the praise of his glory, who first trusted in Christ. In whom ye also trusted, after that ye heard the word of truth, the gospel of your salvation: in whom also after that ye believed, ye were sealed with that holy Spirit of promise, Which is the earnest of our inheritance until the redemption of the purchased possession, unto the praise of his glory.*

Because our surety is covered by the finished work of Christ, and sealed by the ernest payment of the Spirit, we can count on it. 

But for the stranger, we shall smart for it.

<!-- post 17190 · 2026-02-15 07:15 · study -->

Finally, we are also given instruction in character. That gracious women are honorable, but women without discretion are not. That strong men retain their riches and merciful men do themselves good. 

*The wicked worketh a deceitful work: but to him that soweth righteousness shall be a sure reward.* How do we scatter righteousness? Winning souls. 

*The fruit of the righteous is a tree of life; and he that winneth souls is wise.* 

The spiritual application to being one who scattererth, sowing righteousness, winning souls and bearing fruit, not hoarding our corn but blessing others...is again agrarian language as a parable for spreading the gospel. The greatest wisdom and counsel one can share. A blessing to those that receive it, and laying up treasure in Heaven for those that freely give it. 

And this is the promise of God for our efforts; *Behold, the righteous shall be recompensed in the earth: much more the wicked and the sinner.*

## Proverbs 12  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-16

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17191

<!-- post 17191 · 2026-02-16 06:49 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 12**
*Whoso loveth instruction loveth knowledge: but he that hateth reproof is brutish.*

*2 A good man obtaineth favour of the Lord: but a man of wicked devices will he condemn.*

*3 A man shall not be established by wickedness: but the root of the righteous shall not be moved.*

*4 A virtuous woman is a crown to her husband: but she that maketh ashamed is as rottenness in his bones.*

*5 The thoughts of the righteous are right: but the counsels of the wicked are deceit.*

*6 The words of the wicked are to lie in wait for blood: but the mouth of the upright shall deliver them.*

*7 The wicked are overthrown, and are not: but the house of the righteous shall stand.*

*8 A man shall be commended according to his wisdom: but he that is of a perverse heart shall be despised.*

*9 He that is despised, and hath a servant, is better than he that honoureth himself, and lacketh bread.*

*10 A righteous man regardeth the life of his beast: but the tender mercies of the wicked are cruel.*

*11 He that tilleth his land shall be satisfied with bread: but he that followeth vain persons is void of understanding.*

*12 The wicked desireth the net of evil men: but the root of the righteous yieldeth fruit.*

*13 The wicked is snared by the transgression of his lips: but the just shall come out of trouble.*

*14 A man shall be satisfied with good by the fruit of his mouth: and the recompence of a man's hands shall be rendered unto him.*

*15 The way of a fool is right in his own eyes: but he that hearkeneth unto counsel is wise.*

*16 A fool's wrath is presently known: but a prudent man covereth shame.*

*17 He that speaketh truth sheweth forth righteousness: but a false witness deceit.*

*18 There is that speaketh like the piercings of a sword: but the tongue of the wise is health.*

*19 The lip of truth shall be established for ever: but a lying tongue is but for a moment.*

*20 Deceit is in the heart of them that imagine evil: but to the counsellors of peace is joy.*

*21 There shall no evil happen to the just: but the wicked shall be filled with mischief.*

*22 Lying lips are abomination to the Lord: but they that deal truly are his delight.*

*23 A prudent man concealeth knowledge: but the heart of fools proclaimeth foolishness.*

*24 The hand of the diligent shall bear rule: but the slothful shall be under tribute.*

*25 Heaviness in the heart of man maketh it stoop: but a good word maketh it glad.*

*26 The righteous is more excellent than his neighbour: but the way of the wicked seduceth them.*

*27 The slothful man roasteth not that which he took in hunting: but the substance of a diligent man is precious.*

*28 In the way of righteousness is life: and in the pathway thereof there is no death.*

<!-- post 17192 · 2026-02-16 07:07 · study -->

**Proverbs 12** opens with another reminder that loving instruction and knowledge is precious, and the way of righteousness, while hating correction and wisdom is brutish and wicked. 

And verse two reiterates that a good man - the just, wise, fearful of God who seeks after knowledge and walks in His ways, obtains favor from the Lord, but the wicked are condemned. 

**Proverbs 9:10**
*The fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom: and the knowledge of the holy is understanding*.

**Hebrews 11:6**
*But without faith it is impossible to please him: for he that cometh to God must believe that he is, and that he is a rewarder of them that diligently seek him.*

**Romans 10:17**
*So then faith cometh by hearing, and hearing by the word of God.*

**Romans 5:1-2**
*Therefore being justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ: By whom also we have access by faith into this grace wherein we stand, and rejoice in hope of the glory of God.*

**Ephesians 2:8**
*For by grace are ye saved through faith; and that not of yourselves: it is the gift of God*...

We see how a man is made good, by the fear of God and hearing of His word, by faith seeking Him and truth, and by faith we have access to His grace, we obtain the favor of the Lord, receiving His gift of salvation. 

The consistent gospel message of Proverbs cannot be overlooked. 

Wickedness cannot establish a man, but the root of Righteousness, that is Christ Jesus, shall not be moved. He is the "root of Jesse" and a "branch of righteousness" (**Isaiah 11, Jeremiah 23:5-6**), established as the foundational righteousness for all believers. Through faith, believers are grafted into this root, allowing them to bear the fruit of righteousness, by the precious seed of His word.  

Romans 11:16-18 
*For if the firstfruit be holy, the lump is also holy: and if the root be holy, so are the branches. And if some of the branches be broken off, and thou, being a wild olive tree, wert grafted in among them, and with them partakest of the root and fatness of the olive tree; Boast not against the branches. But if thou boast, thou bearest not the root, but the root thee.*

Dispensationalists will claim the root is Israel, but it has always been Christ. Just as Catholics will claim Peter, "a stone," is the rock, which actually is the profession that Jesus is Christ. Because no other foundation can be laid;

**1 Corinthians 3:11**
*For other foundation can no man lay than that is laid, which is Jesus Christ.*

All wisdom leads to Christ.

**Matthew 7:24-25** 
*Therefore whosoever heareth these sayings of mine, and doeth them, I will liken him unto a wise man, which built his house upon a rock: And the rain descended, and the floods came, and the winds blew, and beat upon that house; and it fell not: for it was founded upon a rock.*

<!-- post 17193 · 2026-02-16 07:18 · study -->

Much of the chapter gives a contrast of the righteous and the wicked. The fruit of righteousness produces a stable, life-giving outcome: right and just thoughts, words that deliver and bring deliverance, enduring houses and legacies that stand firm, wisdom that earns commendation, compassionate regard even for beasts, roots that yield true fruit, escape from trouble, satisfaction from good speech and diligent hands, truth that establishes forever, joy for those who counsel peace, protection from evil, delight in the sight of God, excellence over neighbors, and ultimately leading to eternal life without death.

While the fruit of the wicked proves destructive and fleeting: deceitful counsels, words that lie in wait for blood and ensnare through transgression, overthrow and utter disappearance, perverse hearts deserving despise, cruel even in pretended mercy, desire only for evil nets, snaring by their own lips, momentary lying tongues, hearts filled with imagined evil and mischief, abomination through lying lips, seductive ways that lead others astray, and a path ending in no lasting good and death.

We see that even in how we treat animals, livestock, pets, our heart is revealed. And the sad truth that even as we excel over our neighbors by our seeking first the ways of God, many would rather choose to chase lies and false promises of the wicked. 

The diligent and the hard worker will bear rule, have precious substance, while the slothful is ruled over and eats rotten raw meat. That a man hated but established is better than a braggart with nothing to show for it. That a virtuous woman is desired above a shameful woman. 

These lessons of life and how to live it are a blessing of the grace and mercy of the Lord. His words are the rock which we can build upon, knowing it shall never move.

*24 The hand of the diligent shall bear rule: but the slothful shall be under tribute.*

*25 Heaviness in the heart of man maketh it stoop: but a good word maketh it glad.*

*26 The righteous is more excellent than his neighbour: but the way of the wicked seduceth them.*

*27 The slothful man roasteth not that which he took in hunting: but the substance of a diligent man is precious.*

*28 In the way of righteousness is life: and in the pathway thereof there is no death.*

## Proverbs 13  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-17

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17194

<!-- post 17194 · 2026-02-17 06:08 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 13**
*A wise son heareth his father's instruction: but a scorner heareth not rebuke.*

*2 A man shall eat good by the fruit of his mouth: but the soul of the transgressors shall eat violence.*

*3 He that keepeth his mouth keepeth his life: but he that openeth wide his lips shall have destruction.*

*4 The soul of the sluggard desireth, and hath nothing: but the soul of the diligent shall be made fat.*

*5 A righteous man hateth lying: but a wicked man is loathsome, and cometh to shame.*

*6 Righteousness keepeth him that is upright in the way: but wickedness overthroweth the sinner.*

*7 There is that maketh himself rich, yet hath nothing: there is that maketh himself poor, yet hath great riches.*

*8 The ransom of a man's life are his riches: but the poor heareth not rebuke.*

*9 The light of the righteous rejoiceth: but the lamp of the wicked shall be put out.*

*10 Only by pride cometh contention: but with the well advised is wisdom.*

*11 Wealth gotten by vanity shall be diminished: but he that gathereth by labour shall increase.*

*12 Hope deferred maketh the heart sick: but when the desire cometh, it is a tree of life.*

*13 Whoso despiseth the word shall be destroyed: but he that feareth the commandment shall be rewarded.*

*14 The law of the wise is a fountain of life, to depart from the snares of death.*

*15 Good understanding giveth favour: but the way of transgressors is hard.*

*16 Every prudent man dealeth with knowledge: but a fool layeth open his folly.*

*17 A wicked messenger falleth into mischief: but a faithful ambassador is health.*

*18 Poverty and shame shall be to him that refuseth instruction: but he that regardeth reproof shall be honoured.*

*19 The desire accomplished is sweet to the soul: but it is abomination to fools to depart from evil.*

*20 He that walketh with wise men shall be wise: but a companion of fools shall be destroyed.*

*21 Evil pursueth sinners: but to the righteous good shall be repayed.*

*22 A good man leaveth an inheritance to his children's children: and the wealth of the sinner is laid up for the just.*

*23 Much food is in the tillage of the poor: but there is that is destroyed for want of judgment.*

*24 He that spareth his rod hateth his son: but he that loveth him chasteneth him betimes.*

*25 The righteous eateth to the satisfying of his soul: but the belly of the wicked shall want.*

<!-- post 17195 · 2026-02-17 06:33 · study -->

**Proverbs 13** opens and closes with very similar sayings, bookended thoughts that go well together. 

*A wise son heareth his father's instruction...He that spareth his rod hateth his son. A scorner heareth not rebuke...He that loveth* his son *chasteneth him betimes.*

 These concepts all go together. A scorner is that way because his father didn't love him enough to scourge him. A wise son listens because his father cared enough to apply the rod. 

In the same manner, the Lord often applies His wisdom by the rod, and humility and understanding of consequences comes with each stroke. 

**Hebrews 12:4-8** 
*Ye have not yet resisted unto blood, striving against sin. And ye have forgotten the exhortation which speaketh unto you as unto children, My son, despise not thou the chastening of the Lord, nor faint when thou art rebuked of him: For whom the Lord loveth he chasteneth, and scourgeth every son whom he receiveth. If ye endure chastening, God dealeth with you as with sons; for what son is he whom the father chasteneth not? But if ye be without chastisement, whereof all are partakers, then are ye bastards, and not sons.*

God is a loving Father. When we have forgotten the instruction of our parents, when we wander from His paths and neglect His word, we are rebuked of Him. If we aren't, we should wonder are we sons or bastards. Are we wise sons or scorners?

I know from experience that the Lord chasteneth betimes, that is early and as needed. And we should endure it with joy, knowing He is our Father, and take heed to His instructions, as wise children. 

Similarly, verse 2 mirrors verse 25, and you can see why those that applied the numbering for our benefit saw it fitting to cap chapter 12 here. 

*A man shall eat good by the fruit of his mouth: but the soul of the transgressors shall eat violence... The righteous eateth to the satisfying of his soul, but the belly of the wicked shall want.* 

God ties our physical and spiritual fullness and satisfaction to our words; the righteous man speaking goodly words has what they need, but the wicked transgressors receive only violence and destruction, and are ever wanting. *He that keepeth his mouth keepeth his life, but he that openeth wide his lips*? Destruction.

It is a bitter end to the wicked. As the chapter goes on we see the end of their ways is always sure; hunger, shame, overthrown in violence. Their lamp put out. Proud, contentious. The wealth of vanity diminished. A hard way ending in nothing but death. Their folly revealed to all. Subject to mischief, given poverty and shame. Destroyed by their choice of foolish companions. Pursued by evil, their wealth taken to repay the just for the wicked's trespasses. Their savings a ransom for themselves. 

They end up destroyed for want of judgement. They didn't listen to the instruction of their father. They didn't take to the reproof from God.

<!-- post 17196 · 2026-02-17 06:56 · study -->

Now the wise son, who takes instruction and reproof, who learns from the word and the scourging, his end is sweet. A good father who loves him enough to instruct and chasten. A God who does the same. This results in prudence and discretion, diligence in labors, rejecting the way of the sluggards. Hatred of lying, keeping in the upright way, watching their tongue, what proceeds from their lips. 

**Matthew 15:11, 17-20** 
*Not that which goeth into the mouth defileth a man; but that which cometh out of the mouth, this defileth a man...Do not ye yet understand, that whatsoever entereth in at the mouth goeth into the belly, and is cast out into the draught? But those things which proceed out of the mouth come forth from the heart; and they defile the man. For out of the heart proceed evil thoughts, murders, adulteries, fornications, thefts, false witness, blasphemies: These are the things which defile a man: but to eat with unwashen hands defileth not a man.*

The light of the righteous man does shine and those that receive it rejoice, the wisdom of good counsel, rewards all who harken to it. 

*Whoso despiseth the word shall be destroyed: but he that feareth the commandment shall be rewarded.* This gospel theme continues to be hammered in Proverbs, and the spiritual application of this wisdom is undeniable, but the practical meaning cannot be ignored. God tells us over and over of the reward of obedience to His commandments. The long life, the blessings and the abundance of wealth all come to those who are wise and fear the Lord, and His commands. His promises are sure:

*The law of the wise is a fountain of life, to depart from the snares of death.*

This good understanding brings favor. God's grace, making our way easier. The sinners life is hard. 

Diligence is rewarded, but this wise trait is often coupled with the wisdom of delaying gratification. Putting off their desires, knowing a greater reward awaits. Knowing to scatter the seed, in anticipation of a later harvest. Building up stores to the winter, but being generous with what you have, not being too short sighted to miss how that benefits you in the long run. How to wait patiently on the Lord. 

*The desire accomplished is sweet to the soul.*..*Hope deferred maketh the heart sick: but when the desire cometh, it is a tree of life.* Hard work pays off. Quick riches fade but he that gathers by labor gains increase. 

Low time preference is the behavioral tendency to delay immediate gratification in favor of future, long-term rewards. It reflects a patient, disciplined, and forward-thinking mindset that values hard work, prudence, acquiring knowledge and understanding, and it is a sign of wisdom. 

The foolish are sluggards, they have high time preference and are easily tempted into sin. They covet, they seek mischief and violence, they gain their wealth by vanity, only to loose it. When they do store up against the future, they allow their greed to keep them from using it wisely, and it corrupts and rots, it's turned over to the just to recompense for evil, and is stolen or lost - *destroyed for want of judgement.*

<!-- post 17197 · 2026-02-17 07:11 · study -->

This lesson of wisdom and folly being the vision of long term rewards vs immediate gratification creates a seeming contradiction in **Proverbs 13:7**:

*There is that maketh himself rich, yet hath nothing: there is that maketh himself poor, yet hath great riches*.

How can a man make himself rich and have nothing, or poor and have everything? By faith in Christ Jesus. 

**Matthew 5:3**
*Blessed are the poor in spirit: for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.*

**Mark 8:36-37** 
*For what shall it profit a man, if he shall gain the whole world, and lose his own soul? Or what shall a man give in exchange for his soul?*

**Matthew 19:24** 
*And again I say unto you, It is easier for a camel to go through the eye of a needle, than for a rich man to enter into the kingdom of God.*

God is not opposed to earthly riches, to financial gain for His children. Proverbs is filled with calls to diligence and wisdom, that it be well with us and promises of substance and wealth if we do well. 

Job and Abraham and Solomon were incredibly wealthy in their day. It isn't the wealth we gain but what we do with it. Being poor of spirit, that we humble ourselves before the Lord. Being filled with gratitude and delayed satisfaction, liberal and generous with what we have, putting other's profit above our own. 

Being financially poor is not always a sign of wickedness. But the ways of the wicked lead to poverty and shame. The poor heareth not rebuke. But the poor man may be wise and rich in Christ. The rich man may be so by wisdom, but it is harder for one with riches to do what's right. 

It isn't the wealth but the spirit of a man. It isn't their gain but what they do with it. It isn't the riches but where your treasure is. This is not a contradiction, but discernment and Wisdom. Wealth isn't the point, but the willingness to follow the word of God, and to be satisfied with what He provides. 

**Philippians 4:11-13**
*Not that I speak in respect of want: for I have learned, in whatsoever state I am, therewith to be content. I know both how to be abased, and I know how to abound: every where and in all things I am instructed both to be full and to be hungry, both to abound and to suffer need. I can do all things through Christ which strengtheneth me.* Amen!

<!-- post 17198 · 2026-02-17 12:35 · study -->

This is so important for parents to understand. Don't spare the rod!

## Proverbs 14  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-18

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17200

<!-- post 17200 · 2026-02-18 05:47 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 14**
*Every wise woman buildeth her house: but the foolish plucketh it down with her hands.*

*2 He that walketh in his uprightness feareth the Lord: but he that is perverse in his ways despiseth him.*

*3 In the mouth of the foolish is a rod of pride: but the lips of the wise shall preserve them.*

*4 Where no oxen are, the crib is clean: but much increase is by the strength of the ox.*

*5 A faithful witness will not lie: but a false witness will utter lies.*

*6 A scorner seeketh wisdom, and findeth it not: but knowledge is easy unto him that understandeth.*

*7 Go from the presence of a foolish man, when thou perceivest not in him the lips of knowledge.*

*8 The wisdom of the prudent is to understand his way: but the folly of fools is deceit.*

*9 Fools make a mock at sin: but among the righteous there is favour.*

*10 The heart knoweth his own bitterness; and a stranger doth not intermeddle with his joy.*

*11 The house of the wicked shall be overthrown: but the tabernacle of the upright shall flourish.*

*12 There is a way which seemeth right unto a man, but the end thereof are the ways of death.*

*13 Even in laughter the heart is sorrowful; and the end of that mirth is heaviness.*

*14 The backslider in heart shall be filled with his own ways: and a good man shall be satisfied from himself.*

*15 The simple believeth every word: but the prudent man looketh well to his going.*

*16 A wise man feareth, and departeth from evil: but the fool rageth, and is confident.*

*17 He that is soon angry dealeth foolishly: and a man of wicked devices is hated.*

*18 The simple inherit folly: but the prudent are crowned with knowledge.*

*19 The evil bow before the good; and the wicked at the gates of the righteous.*

*20 The poor is hated even of his own neighbour: but the rich hath many friends.*

*21 He that despiseth his neighbour sinneth: but he that hath mercy on the poor, happy is he.*

*22 Do they not err that devise evil? but mercy and truth shall be to them that devise good.*

*23 In all labour there is profit: but the talk of the lips tendeth only to penury.*

*24 The crown of the wise is their riches: but the foolishness of fools is folly.*

*25 A true witness delivereth souls: but a deceitful witness speaketh lies.*

*26 In the fear of the Lord is strong confidence: and his children shall have a place of refuge.*

*27 The fear of the Lord is a fountain of life, to depart from the snares of death.*

*28 In the multitude of people is the king's honour: but in the want of people is the destruction of the prince.*

*29 He that is slow to wrath is of great understanding: but he that is hasty of spirit exalteth folly.*

*30 A sound heart is the life of the flesh: but envy the rottenness of the bones.*

*31 He that oppresseth the poor reproacheth his Maker: but he that honoureth him hath mercy on the poor.*

*32 The wicked is driven away in his wickedness: but the righteous hath hope in his death.*

*33 Wisdom resteth in the heart of him that hath understanding: but that which is in the midst of fools is made known.*

*34 Righteousness exalteth a nation: but sin is a reproach to any people.*

*35 The king's favour is toward a wise servant: but his wrath is against him that causeth shame.*

<!-- post 17201 · 2026-02-18 06:19 · study -->

Continuing in **Proverbs 14**, we are given several wise sayings, often repeating the points to really drive them home. Archetypes of wisdom contrasted with the folly of fools and warnings against liars. 

The wise builder, embodied especially in the "wise woman" of verse 1 who "*buildeth her house*," representing constructive, diligent wisdom that strengthens family, home, and community, where the foolish woman destroys her home, as plucking a hen. Bit by bit. 

Again, we are told the upright fear the Lord. This healthy respect for God is the source and substance of wisdom. But those on the crooked ways of the perverse hat the Lord. 

They have been made to know the truth, by the wrath of God revealed from Heaven against the ungodly, but they hate Him and truth. 

**Romans 1:28**
*And even as they did not like to retain God in their knowledge, God gave them over to a reprobate mind, to do those things which are not convenient*

God will give the wicked what they desire, and by rejecting them utterly, give them over to their perverse ways to their own destruction. 

The fool speaks out because of pride, but the wise keeps their lips quiet when insulted. This is a hard one for many, including myself. Hard to remain quiet when being falsely accused and raised on or our pride is attacked. But Jesus showed us the way;

**Matthew 27:11-14** 
*And Jesus stood before the governor: and the governor asked him, saying, Art thou the King of the Jews? And Jesus said unto him, Thou sayest. And when he was accused of the chief priests and elders, he answered nothing. Then said Pilate unto him, Hearest thou not how many things they witness against thee? And he answered him to never a word; insomuch that the governor marvelled greatly.*

Humility in the face of accusations is often the most potent defense. Counterintuitive but like much of God's wisdom, it goes against man's understanding. 

*Where no oxen are, the crib is clean: but much increase is by the strength of the ox.*

There are tradeoffs in life, and we are better for it. Sometimes, to get things done you have to be willing to shovel the dung. Another example of delaying gratification to your betterment. 

Faithful witnesses do not lie, but a false witness will. It seems obvious, and repeated in verse 25: *A true witness delivereth souls: but a deceitful witness speaketh lies.* When given the opportunity, we should remember the deliverance we received, and speak truth to deliver others. This is pleasing to God and foundational in the 10 Commandments. Thou shall not bear false witness. 

*A scorner seeketh wisdom and findeth it not...* reminds me of the false teacher, a reprobate who Paul describes in **2 Timothy 3:7** *Ever learning, and never able to come to the knowledge of the truth.*

How can they, without the fear of God? How can they when they scoff at truth and hold it in unrighteousness? When they hate God rather than fear Him?

So we should reject the foolish man when you perceive he does not have knowledge. Again, flee a false prophet or someone claiming to know who just spouts nonsense. They ultimately will lie and speak falsehoods and defend their pride. They make a mock of sin, joking about evil ways. Flee these people. Don't take company with them, and don't try to sift any knowledge from them. They may be right about something, but in the end it isn't worth the rest that comes with it. 

*The heart knoweth his own bitterness; and a stranger doth not intermeddle with his joy.*

No one but you and God can know the heartache or the joy you feel in your heart. Truly know it. And you can't know another's, even when you empathize or rejoice with them. It's an important thing to keep in mind when others deal with you, or you're dealing with others. Have grace because of this limitation, and thank God for it, too. If our heart were open to men, it could be embarrassing and shameful. Thank God we don't actually wear our hearts on our sleeve.

<!-- post 17202 · 2026-02-18 06:27 · study -->

Again, in verse 11 we see the wise builder's tabernacle flourishes when the wicked's will be overthrown. Not the structure, tent of building but the nature of the home. The family. 

*There is a way which seemeth right unto a man, but the end thereof are the ways of death.* God's ways are not our ways. Our heart is deceitful and wicked. The things we think are right or the things of God we see as counterintuitive, often lacks perspective. Longview. Or are based on lies we tell ourselves to justify our sin. Prideful and graceless. Even the things we may laugh at, the Bible says, our heart remains sorrowful. Even in mirth is heaviness. Who can know man's heart?

And so, *The backslider in heart shall be filled with his own ways: and a good man shall be satisfied from himself.* When we fall into sin, leaving the good path of the Lord, backslidding from whatever progress we have made, not walking in the Spirit but our own desires, we are filled with our own judgement.

*A sound heart is the life of the flesh: but envy the rottenness of the bones.*

We should be mindful of our heart, to keep it sound. To limit what we give into when we heed it. To know the spirit where what we desire comes from.

<!-- post 17203 · 2026-02-18 06:49 · study -->

Remember the poor. Do not despise them, as we are poor in spirit and God gave to us abundantly. As Jesus said;

**Matthew 25:35-40**
*For I was an hungred, and ye gave me meat: I was thirsty, and ye gave me drink: I was a stranger, and ye took me in: Naked, and ye clothed me: I was sick, and ye visited me: I was in prison, and ye came unto me. Then shall the righteous answer him, saying, Lord, when saw we thee an hungred, and fed thee? or thirsty, and gave thee drink? When saw we thee a stranger, and took thee in? or naked, and clothed thee? Or when saw we thee sick, or in prison, and came unto thee? And the King shall answer and say unto them, Verily I say unto you, Inasmuch as ye have done it unto one of the least of these my brethren, ye have done it unto me.*

And the promises of God to repay, that when we give to the poor we lend to the Lord.

**Psalms 41:1**
*Blessed is he that considereth the poor: the LORD will deliver him in time of trouble.*

And again, in verse 32 we see the gospel in **Proverbs**, the righteous has hope in our death. That blessed hope of eternal life in Christ Jesus. This is wisdom, to hear and believe the truth of God's mercy and grace, by the imputed righteousness of Christ. 

Because righteousness exalteth a nation and sin is a reproach to any people, we should strive to win souls, instruct wisdom, have standards, that our nation would be blessed by our righteousness in Christ. Not Christian nationalism but a nation of Christians.

## Proverbs 14  ·  Other post on this chapter  ·  2022-06-13

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/2643

<!-- post 2643 · 2022-06-13 06:25 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 14**

*Every wise woman buildeth her house: but the foolish plucketh it down with her hands.*

*2 He that walketh in his uprightness feareth the Lord: but he that is perverse in his ways despiseth him.*

*9 Fools make a mock at sin: but among the righteous there is favour.*

*10 The heart knoweth his own bitterness; and a stranger doth not intermeddle with his joy.*

*11 The house of the wicked shall be overthrown: but the tabernacle of the upright shall flourish.*

*12 There is a way which seemeth right unto a man, but the end thereof are the ways of death.*

*13 Even in laughter the heart is sorrowful; and the end of that mirth is heaviness.*

*14 The backslider in heart shall be filled with his own ways: and a good man shall be satisfied from himself.*

*15 The simple believeth every word: but the prudent man looketh well to his going.*

*16 A wise man feareth, and departeth from evil: but the fool rageth, and is confident.*

*17 He that is soon angry dealeth foolishly: and a man of wicked devices is hated.*

*18 The simple inherit folly: but the prudent are crowned with knowledge.*

*19 The evil bow before the good; and the wicked at the gates of the righteous.*

*20 The poor is hated even of his own neighbour: but the rich hath many friends.*

*21 He that despiseth his neighbour sinneth: but he that hath mercy on the poor, happy is he.*

*22 Do they not err that devise evil? but mercy and truth shall be to them that devise good.*

*23 In all labour there is profit: but the talk of the lips tendeth only to penury.*

*24 The crown of the wise is their riches: but the foolishness of fools is folly.*

*25 A true witness delivereth souls: but a deceitful witness speaketh lies.*

*26 In the fear of the Lord is strong confidence: and his children shall have a place of refuge.*

*27 The fear of the Lord is a fountain of life, to depart from the snares of death.*

*28 In the multitude of people is the king's honour: but in the want of people is the destruction of the prince.*

*29 He that is slow to wrath is of great understanding: but he that is hasty of spirit exalteth folly.*

*30 A sound heart is the life of the flesh: but envy the rottenness of the bones.*

*31 He that oppresseth the poor reproacheth his Maker: but he that honoureth him hath mercy on the poor.*

*32 The wicked is driven away in his wickedness: but the righteous hath hope in his death.*

*33 Wisdom resteth in the heart of him that hath understanding: but that which is in the midst of fools is made known.*

*34 Righteousness exalteth a nation: but sin is a reproach to any people.*

*35 The king's favour is toward a wise servant: but his wrath is against him that causeth shame.*

## Proverbs 15  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-02-20

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17205

<!-- post 17205 · 2026-02-20 06:19 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 15**
*A soft answer turneth away wrath: but grievous words stir up anger.*

*2 The tongue of the wise useth knowledge aright: but the mouth of fools poureth out foolishness.*

*3 The eyes of the Lord are in every place, beholding the evil and the good.*

*4 A wholesome tongue is a tree of life: but perverseness therein is a breach in the spirit.*

*5 A fool despiseth his father's instruction: but he that regardeth reproof is prudent.*

*6 In the house of the righteous is much treasure: but in the revenues of the wicked is trouble.*

*7 The lips of the wise disperse knowledge: but the heart of the foolish doeth not so.*

*8 The sacrifice of the wicked is an abomination to the Lord: but the prayer of the upright is his delight.*

*9 The way of the wicked is an abomination unto the Lord: but he loveth him that followeth after righteousness.*

*10 Correction is grievous unto him that forsaketh the way: and he that hateth reproof shall die.*

*11 Hell and destruction are before the Lord: how much more then the hearts of the children of men?*

*12 A scorner loveth not one that reproveth him: neither will he go unto the wise.*

*13 A merry heart maketh a cheerful countenance: but by sorrow of the heart the spirit is broken.*

*14 The heart of him that hath understanding seeketh knowledge: but the mouth of fools feedeth on foolishness.*

*15 All the days of the afflicted are evil: but he that is of a merry heart hath a continual feast.*

*16 Better is little with the fear of the Lord than great treasure and trouble therewith.*

*17 Better is a dinner of herbs where love is, than a stalled ox and hatred therewith.*

*18 A wrathful man stirreth up strife: but he that is slow to anger appeaseth strife.*

*19 The way of the slothful man is as an hedge of thorns: but the way of the righteous is made plain.*

*20 A wise son maketh a glad father: but a foolish man despiseth his mother.*

*21 Folly is joy to him that is destitute of wisdom: but a man of understanding walketh uprightly.*

*22 Without counsel purposes are disappointed: but in the multitude of counsellors they are established.*

*23 A man hath joy by the answer of his mouth: and a word spoken in due season, how good is it!*

*24 The way of life is above to the wise, that he may depart from hell beneath.*

*25 The Lord will destroy the house of the proud: but he will establish the border of the widow.*

*26 The thoughts of the wicked are an abomination to the Lord: but the words of the pure are pleasant words.*

*27 He that is greedy of gain troubleth his own house; but he that hateth gifts shall live.*

*28 The heart of the righteous studieth to answer: but the mouth of the wicked poureth out evil things.*

*29 The Lord is far from the wicked: but he heareth the prayer of the righteous.*

*30 The light of the eyes rejoiceth the heart: and a good report maketh the bones fat.*

*31 The ear that heareth the reproof of life abideth among the wise.*

*32 He that refuseth instruction despiseth his own soul: but he that heareth reproof getteth understanding.*

*33 The fear of the Lord is the instruction of wisdom; and before honour is humility.*

<!-- post 17206 · 2026-02-20 06:37 · study -->

**Proverbs 15** begins by again establishing the truth that things we say, and the manner we say it, is a key instruction for holy living. 

A soft answer, given in kindness and grace can calm a situation. The tongue of the wise uses knowledge and disperses it. 

A wholesome tongue, speaking pure words is a tree of life. Both spiritually, by the gospel, but also metaphorically by lifting up others' spirit. Plans and purposes rely on wise counsel, and many counselors make them established. 

How we use our mouth, the words we choose, the knowledge we impart, the instruction we give, all is ultimately judged by God. 

**Matthew 12:36**
*But I say unto you, That every idle word that men shall speak, they shall give account thereof in the day of judgment*.

Because of this, the grievous words that engender strife, stir up anger, or the mouth that pours out foolishness, all is ultimately vanity, and the man who hasn't humbled himself to the Lord, and received justification through Christ will surely be ashamed at the judgment. Imagine every foolish or wicked thing you've ever said in anger, read aloud by the holy God for all to hear. 

Every perverse word that causes others' spirit to fall. Every ignorant thing that led others astray. Every contention and strife stirred up by our unwise words, lack of discretion and wisdom, all revealed from the Great White Throne. 

I thank God for His great salvation, that spares me from such utter humiliation. This alone should convict man to repentance towards the gospel.

We see that even the thoughts of the wicked are an abomination to God, but pure words are pleasing to Him. A soft answer and pure words, instruction and knowledge, these please the Lord, and they are good to our soul, and are a blessing to the people in our life. 

*A man hath joy by the answer of his mouth: and a word spoken in due season, how good is it!*

How good it is to hear the joy of a brother when we are feeling down! The comfort in our mourning, being told exactly the thing we needed to hear, with all due wisdom and counsel, this is a gift to all, one we should spread ourselves.

<!-- post 17207 · 2026-02-20 06:50 · study -->

The words we say, the things that our tongue forms and passes our lips, is a window to our heart. 

**Matthew 12:34**
*O generation of vipers, how can ye, being evil, speak good things? for out of the abundance of the heart the mouth speaketh*.

The heart of the fool prevents their lips from speaking wisdom. The heart one without understanding seeks foolishness to fill it, rather than understanding. The righteous' heart studies that they may give good answer to those seeking wisdom, but the wicked heart causes evil things to pour out the mouth of the fool. 

Every thought and word is known by God. 

*The eyes of the Lord are in every place, beholding the evil and the good.*

The Lord is omnipotent and omniscient, ever present and knowing all things. Not only the words we speak, but the heart we keep. 

*Hell and destruction are before the Lord: how much more then the hearts of the children of men?*

If the dead and hell are tormented in the presence of the Lamb, before God night and day, how much more our hearts are known by Him? 

**Psalm 44:21**
*Shall not God search this out? for he knoweth the secrets of the heart.*

The heart rejoices in the light of the eyes, a message of good report. Being the bearer of glad tidings, the reproof of life, the gospel...all this serves to brighten the heart of many, if they are inclined to wisdom and seek truth. Of course the fool rejects reproof and correction, to their shame.

<!-- post 17208 · 2026-02-20 07:07 · study -->

The constant reminder from God to heed the wisdom of your father, to incline your ear to instruction, to prudently regard reproof continues to be hammered home. 

Following after the way of righteousness is wise, but those that have forsaken the way of God despise correction and hate reproof. Scorners hate being reproved of their ignorance. They won't seek after the wise. They feed on foolishness, are destitute of wisdom, their plans come to naught for lack of counsel, those greedy of gain and their pride brings destruction to their house.  

*The sacrifice of the wicked is an abomination to the Lord: but the prayer of the upright is his delight.*

*The Lord is far from the wicked: but he heareth the prayer of the righteous.*

Having God on your side, accepting your prayers, working in your life is a treasure beyond measure. The wicked and their public display of piety, their self-righteous works and so-called fasts, the "sacrifices" they make, be it the Old Testament animal offerings, or the vows made today to "cut out sweets," or to "drink less" or whatever, are an abomination to the Lord. 

*The fear of the Lord is the instruction of wisdom; and before honour is humility*. The wicked may have a zeal for God, but not according to knowledge. They lack the humility that comes before wisdom. 

**Romans 10:3**
*For they being ignorant of God's righteousness, and going about to establish their own righteousness, have not submitted themselves unto the righteousness of God.*

The humility of submitting to His righteousness, the fear of the Lord, that we may gain understanding, being willing to receive reproof and correction, to seek after knowledge and study to be approved before God, that we may have a ready answer to all for our hope, this is the wisdom and the way we must seek. 

*The way of life is above to the wise, that he may depart from hell beneath.*

<!-- post 17209 · 2026-02-20 07:16 · study -->

The promises of God towards His children are unfailing. That in our house will be much treasure, but not necessarily riches and wealth of this world. So rejoice! 

Our days with a merry heart are a continual feast. *Better is little with fear of the Lord than great treasure and trouble* *therewith*, Solomon writes. *Better is a dinner of herbs where love is, than a stalled ox and hatred therewith*. 

The slothful fool's way is made harder, like a hedge of thorns to get through. The wicked and greedy see their own destruction and live in constant fear and trouble. 

Choose the path of life. The way of God, by humbling yourself to His wisdom and hearing the instruction of your father, and bring that light to others. Be a wise son:

*A wise son maketh a glad father: but a foolish man despiseth his mother*.
