## Proverbs 28  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-04-06

**Proverbs 28**
*The wicked flee when no man pursueth: but the righteous are bold as a lion.*

*2 For the transgression of a land many are the princes thereof: but by a man of understanding and knowledge the state thereof shall be prolonged.*

*3 A poor man that oppresseth the poor is like a sweeping rain which leaveth no food.*

*4 They that forsake the law praise the wicked: but such as keep the law contend with them.*

*5 Evil men understand not judgment: but they that seek the Lord understand all things.*

*6 Better is the poor that walketh in his uprightness, than he that is perverse in his ways, though he be rich.*

*7 Whoso keepeth the law is a wise son: but he that is a companion of riotous men shameth his father.*

*8 He that by usury and unjust gain increaseth his substance, he shall gather it for him that will pity the poor.*

*9 He that turneth away his ear from hearing the law, even his prayer shall be abomination.*

*10 Whoso causeth the righteous to go astray in an evil way, he shall fall himself into his own pit: but the upright shall have good things in possession.*

*11 The rich man is wise in his own conceit; but the poor that hath understanding searcheth him out.*

*12 When righteous men do rejoice, there is great glory: but when the wicked rise, a man is hidden.*

*13 He that covereth his sins shall not prosper: but whoso confesseth and forsaketh them shall have mercy.*

*14 Happy is the man that feareth alway: but he that hardeneth his heart shall fall into mischief.*

*15 As a roaring lion, and a ranging bear; so is a wicked ruler over the poor people.*

*16 The prince that wanteth understanding is also a great oppressor: but he that hateth covetousness shall prolong his days.*

*17 A man that doeth violence to the blood of any person shall flee to the pit; let no man stay him.*

*18 Whoso walketh uprightly shall be saved: but he that is perverse in his ways shall fall at once.*

*19 He that tilleth his land shall have plenty of bread: but he that followeth after vain persons shall have poverty enough.*

*20 A faithful man shall abound with blessings: but he that maketh haste to be rich shall not be innocent.*

*21 To have respect of persons is not good: for for a piece of bread that man will transgress.*

*22 He that hasteth to be rich hath an evil eye, and considereth not that poverty shall come upon him.*

*23 He that rebuketh a man afterwards shall find more favour than he that flattereth with the tongue.*

*24 Whoso robbeth his father or his mother, and saith, It is no transgression; the same is the companion of a destroyer.*

*25 He that is of a proud heart stirreth up strife: but he that putteth his trust in the Lord shall be made fat.*

*26 He that trusteth in his own heart is a fool: but whoso walketh wisely, he shall be delivered.*

*27 He that giveth unto the poor shall not lack: but he that hideth his eyes shall have many a curse.*

*28 When the wicked rise, men hide themselves: but when they perish, the righteous increase.*

<!-- post 17368 · 2026-04-06 06:37 · study -->

**Proverbs 28** continues the sayings of Solomon, recorded by the order of his 10th-generation grandson, King Hezekiah. And it begins with an often used saying; *The wicked flee when no man pursueth*...

The guilty fear the wrath they know they deserve. Constantly looking over their shoulder, and quick to defend themselves where no accusations have been made. Paranoia strikes the thief and the murderer, the habitual criminal lives in constant worry of being found out to the point they will expose themselves when there is no threat.

Parents are familiar with this behavior in guilty children. God is used to this in His children as well. 

*but the righteous are bold as a lion.*

This boldness comes from having a clean conscience towards God by the blood of Christ;

**Hebrews 10:16-22**
*This is the covenant that I will make with them after those days, saith the Lord, I will put my laws into their hearts, and in their minds will I write them; And their sins and iniquities will I remember no more. Now where remission of these is, there is no more offering for sin. Having therefore, brethren, boldness to enter into the holiest by the blood of Jesus, By a new and living way, which he hath consecrated for us, through the veil, that is to say, his flesh; And having an high priest over the house of God; Let us draw near with a true heart in full assurance of faith, having our hearts sprinkled from an evil conscience, and our bodies washed with pure water.*

For God treats us as His children, by faith in Christ, that when we sin, we have forgiveness.

**Hebrews 4:16**
*Let us therefore come boldly unto the throne of grace, that we may obtain mercy, and find grace to help in time of need.*

We don't need to keep a guilty conscience, in constant anxiety. We have the boldness of Christ's righteousness and the promise of a merciful Father 

The righteous don't despise the chastening of the Lord, or faint when we are rebuked of Him;

**Hebrews 12:6-8** 
*For whom the Lord loveth he chasteneth, and scourgeth every son whom he receiveth. If ye endure chastening, God dealeth with you as with sons; for what son is he whom the father chasteneth not? But if ye be without chastisement, whereof all are partakers, then are ye bastards, and not sons.*

But the unbelieving allow the fear of their guilt and unrighteousness overcome them, and their love of sin betray and snare them, they flee the throne of God to hide from His vengeance. Especially those that have heard His word and the instruction of righteousness, and have rejected it.

**Hebrews 10:26-27**
*For if we sin wilfully after that we have received the knowledge of the truth, there remaineth no more sacrifice for sins, But a certain fearful looking for of judgment and fiery indignation, which shall devour the adversaries.*

<!-- post 17371 · 2026-04-07 06:03 · study -->

*For the transgression of a land many are the princes thereof: but by a man of understanding and knowledge the state thereof shall be prolonged*.

There is no political solution for a spiritual problem. The iniquity and sin in a country results in *many princes,* that is more and more oppression and rules and rulers. 

It could also mean instability and high turnover. Frequent coups. Parties switching off power every few years. Revolution after revolution. A sinful people get the leadership they deserve. 

While a wise king, filled with the fear of the Lord and the understanding and knowledge that follow, his rule shall be established and stand, and is the joy of a righteous people. 

Reminder that God has given the responsibility of preserving our nation to Christians. 

**2 Chronicles 7:14**
*If my people, which are called by my name, shall humble themselves, and pray, and seek my face, and turn from their wicked ways; then will I hear from heaven, and will forgive their sin, and will heal their land.*

Revival begins in the heart of believers, in the pews of their local church, in the highways and hedges, at the doorstep of the lost. 

Not at a ballot box, not on cable news, or a podcast or YouTube comments section. It doesn't begin in a Telegram chat, or social media post, or at a rally for a politician. 

Christians are the salt of the earth, preserving their land, prolonging the state of a land. 

**Matthew 5:13**
*Ye are the salt of the earth: but if the salt have lost his savour, wherewith shall it be salted? it is thenceforth good for nothing, but to be cast out, and to be trodden under foot of men.*

<!-- post 17372 · 2026-04-07 06:41 · study -->

What follows, from verse 3 though 28, is a litany of sayings that contrast the wicked, the rich without understanding, the haters of the law, the violent and bloody man, with the faithful, upright, keepers of the law. The prince of understanding, the righteous man, these see their fortunes and their reputation in increase, while their counterparts are undone by their sin. 

We see that a poor man who helps oppress the poor is a threat to all, and also self-destructive. 

That those who foresake the law will praise the wicked, but this that keep the law will contend with them. Those law-haters out there, that make grace an opportunity to justify their sin, will inevitably go after the praise of the wicked. We see it all the time. It begins with excusing themselves from God's precepts, and extends to then justifying sodomites and degenerates. Female led churches with rainbow flags, glorifying rioting and rebellion, and teaching abominations. 

Because evil men understand not judgment, they are perverse and so oppose any mention of what God says, to chase after their own lusts. "God wouldn't want the death penalty!" or "A loving God wouldn't send anyone to Hell!" Those that seek the Lord and in all our ways acknowledge Him, understand the need for love being without dissimulation. Why we must abhor that which is evil. 

We are reminded again it is better to be poor and walking in God's ways, than to be rich and wicked. 

A wise son keeps the law and is a joy to his parents, but the riotous and rebellious son is a shame to his father. 

**Romans 13:13**-**14**
*Let us walk honestly, as in the day; not in rioting and drunkenness, not in chambering and wantonness, not in strife and envying.* *But put ye on the Lord Jesus Christ, and make not provision for the flesh, to fulfil the lusts thereof.*

As Christians, we are to give honor, tribute and custom to whom it is due. To obey the higher authority and the powers that be. Not seek cause for lawlessness and giving the wicked praise because they cause the righteous to go astray unto evil.

<!-- post 17374 · 2026-04-08 07:05 · study -->

Verse 8 cautions against usury and unjust gain, linking the two. Much is made about what the Bible means by usury. Some suggest that a little interest for a loan is fine, that it's just prohibited to charge high or "predatory" interest rates. 

But, in the King James Bible, usury is defined as any interest, profit, or premium charged on a loan, not just excessive or illegal interest as understood in modern terms. When servitude could be the result of not keeping the terms of the agreement, compounding interest on top of the principle could be the difference between slavery or liberty. Seeking to profit off another's need is unjust gain. 

Redemption is the freeing of the burden of debt, paying what's owed and liberating oneself or another from owing something. The gospel language of Christ setting us at liberty from the wages of sin through the gift of eternal life, where the Spirit acts as the surety for the promise of God, that He will not impute our sins against us, but rather apply the payment of Jesus' blood, is all quite deliberate. 

So the idea of adding burdens to the needy that make their ability to pay someone back fairly is an abomination to the Lord. He is gracious and expects us to be gracious as well. 

God's law instituted a "Sabbath year." Every seven years, a "release" was mandated, where creditors were required to cancel debts owed by their fellow Israelites.

**Deuteronomy 15:1-2**
*At the end of every seven years thou shalt make a release. And this is the manner of the release: Every creditor that lendeth ought unto his neighbour shall release it; he shall not exact it of his neighbour, or of his brother; because it is called the Lord's release.*

Servitude could often be a means of survival for the poor, and a blessing. Having a way to work off that which is owed, without interest was a fair system, and limiting the debt to what could be repaid in 7 years not only kept this process from exploiting the poor, but made lenders more cautious in issuing loans. 

But some have an evil heart, and their love of money tempts them. And so the Lord warns;

**Deuteronomy 15:9**
*Beware that there be not a thought in thy wicked heart, saying, The seventh year, the year of release, is at hand; and thine eye be evil against thy poor brother, and thou givest him nought; and he cry unto the Lord against thee, and it be sin unto thee.*

Not all debt was for money. Some servitude was to obtain favor or other gain. 

In **Genesis 29**, Laban tricks Jacob into a total of 14 years of labor for the honor of marriage to Rachel, Laban's younger daughter. After Jacob serves seven years, Laban switches his daughters on the wedding night, sending his older daughter, Leah, to the bridal bed instead of Rachel. Laban justifies this deceit by claiming it is custom for the younger to marry after the older, but he sought to keep Jacob bound to service, exploiting his love for Rachel. 

In addition to the 7 year release, God commands a jubilee every 50 years;

**Leviticus 25:10-13**
*And ye shall hallow the fiftieth year, and proclaim liberty throughout all the land unto all the inhabitants thereof: it shall be a jubile unto you; and ye shall return every man unto his possession, and ye shall return every man unto his family. A jubile shall that fiftieth year be unto you: ye shall not sow, neither reap that which groweth of itself in it, nor gather the grapes in it of thy vine undressed. For it is the jubile; it shall be holy unto you: ye shall eat the increase thereof out of the field. In the year of this jubile ye shall return every man unto his possession.*

The purpose here was to restore families to their land, and set free the captive, that generational poverty could be prevented. Stopping a lending class of rich men from exerting permanent control over the poor. 

If lending to a poor neighbor, one was forbidden from charging usury at all;

**Exodus 22:25**
*If thou lend money to any of my people that is poor by thee, thou shalt not be to him as an usurer, neither shalt thou lay upon him usury.*

<!-- post 17375 · 2026-04-08 07:05 · study -->

This even extended to making a profit on food sold to the poor;

**Leviticus 25:35-37**
*And if thy brother be waxen poor, and fallen in decay with thee; then thou shalt relieve him: yea, though he be a stranger, or a sojourner; that he may live with thee. Take thou no usury of him, or increase: but fear thy God; that thy brother may live with thee. Thou shalt not give him thy money upon usury, nor lend him thy victuals for increase.*

Land owners were expected to allow gleaning of their fields, that after a harvest, the poor should go through the fields and bring in anything left behind by the laborers. 

Alms and feasts of charity are expected of the Lord's people. Forgiveness of debts. A cheerful giver, providing for those in need is praised. 

**Proverbs 11:25-27**
*The liberal soul shall be made fat: and he that watereth shall be watered also himself. He that withholdeth corn, the people shall curse him: but blessing shall be upon the head of him that selleth it.*

As **Proverbs 28:8** promises; 

*He that by usury and unjust gain increaseth his substance, he shall gather it for him that will pity the poor*.

The gain you may make is temporary. The Lord will make certain your wealth is distributed to the righteous, in the end.

Because God is so just to forgive us, we should love one another enough to be like Him.

**Luke 7:41-42**
*There was a certain creditor which had two debtors: the one owed five hundred pence, and the other fifty. And when they had nothing to pay, he frankly forgave them both. Tell me therefore, which of them will love him most?*

So, we should not seek unjust gain, and further, we should relieve those in our debt, as God has forgiven us. 

**Matthew 6:9-13**
*After this manner therefore pray ye: Our Father which art in heaven, Hallowed be thy name. Thy kingdom come, Thy will be done in earth, as it is in heaven. Give us this day our daily bread. And forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors. And lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil: For thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, for ever. Amen.*

*18 Now that which was prepared for me daily was one ox and six choice sheep; also fowls were prepared for me, and once in ten days store of all sorts of wine: yet for all this required not I the bread of the governor, because the bondage was heavy upon this people.*

<!-- post 17377 · 2026-04-08 08:45 · study -->

The covenant God made with the Israelites, that by the works of the law they may know Him, and they would be His people , and He would be their God, put the people in bondage. Because by the same law comes the works of death. 

For Christ to then set them that believe at liberty, by the grace of the Father to not hold us to that covenant, but by the blood of Christ being a new and better testament, built on better promises, that we have been reconciled despite our sin. Despite our own transgression of the law. That God commendeth His love towards us that while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us. This is that forgiveness of debt. This is that grace and mercy that He has for us, and commands us to give towards others.

<!-- post 17382 · 2026-04-11 08:21 · study -->

**Proverbs 28** continues. 

**Verse 9**; *He that turneth away his ear from hearing the law, even his prayer shall be abomination.*

The law-haters out there, who allow the reality of grace and reconciliation in Christ to then turn their ear from the hearing of the law, should consider this saying. Their prayers are an abomination to God. 

To take our liberty and use it as a cover for sin, is wicked. Rather it is a cause to cleanse ourselves, as much as possible, and to not lead those weak in the faith to stumble.  

**1 Corinthians 10:23-24** 
*All things are lawful for me, but all things are not expedient: all things are lawful for me, but all things edify not. Let no man seek his own, but every man another's wealth.*

We should be a model of the law, that it convicts the unsaved, and serves to edify our brethren, weak in the faith, to do better. Not as a stumbling block or a snare, but as an example. Not being offended at the hearing of the law, but affirming it, that it may bring others to Christ. That our prayers are acceptable to the Lord. 

You might not be perfect, and we all fall short, but preaching against sin, especially the sin you still do, shouldn't offend you. It should convict. It should elicit an even hardier "Amen!" 

It's no wonder that **verse 10** follows;

*Whoso causeth the righteous to go astray in an evil way, he shall fall himself into his own pit: but the upright shall have good things in possession.*

**Romans 14** warns of causing a brother to sin by your liberty. 

**Romans 14:14-16;21** 
*I know, and am persuaded by the Lord Jesus, that there is nothing unclean of itself: but to him that esteemeth any thing to be unclean, to him it is unclean. But if thy brother be grieved with thy meat, now walkest thou not charitably. Destroy not him with thy meat, for whom Christ died. Let not then your good be evil spoken of*... *It is good neither to eat flesh, nor to drink wine, nor any thing whereby thy brother stumbleth, or is offended, or is made weak.*

If matters of conscience are left to us to determine, yet we should defer to abstain for the edification of those weak in faith, how much more so the clear things of the law which are sin? 

So, those who take liberty to be libertine, and who teach that the redundant term "free grace" means "God isn't mad at your sin," to then allow fornication, drunkenness and the like, misleading others, will themselves fall victim to their sin. God will repay. 

And those that deliberately bring offense, who corrupt and pervert others into sin, they will fall themselves into the pit they've dug for others. They will be snared by their own trap. 

Better to have standards, to teach the whole counsel of God. To love the law and delight in it, even at liberty by grace. To put the profit of others before your own lusts and desires. To not give others cause to blaspheme the Lord. For the upright shall have good things in possession. They shall be blessed.

<!-- post 17386 · 2026-04-12 07:28 · study -->

Picking up the chapter study at Proverbs **28:1**0;

*The rich man is wise in his own conceit; but the poor that hath understanding searcheth him out.*

We all know the story of the rich young man and Jesus, where he claimed to Christ himself that he had observed all commandments from his youth, and Jesus told him to sell all his things for the poor, and he went away unsaved. 

**Matthew 19:24**
*And again I say unto you, It is easier for a camel to go through the eye of a needle, than for a rich man to enter into the kingdom of God.*

Earlier Proverbs told us some of the reasons this is true. Their riches are their strong tower. They place their trust in things.

But, like the rich young man, they are also wise in their own conceits. He thought he had it all figured out. Those that are well off financially often see themselves, their own wisdom and discernment, as the cause for their success., and their wealth as evidence of their righteousness. A feedback loop that keeps them from even questioning the truth. The rich young man sought to be validated by the "good master," and it didn't occur to him that there is none good but God, that the man Christ Jesus sitting there in front of him was the Lord. 

Out soul-winning, you encounter far more pushback to the gospel among the wealthy. Even in a more modest neighborhood, if you find a home with a pool, motorsport vehicles, a boat, sports car, or some other sign of wealth, you're likely to great a scoffer at the door. 

But the poor? So many are seeking to know the truth. They have nothing else to cling to. Their lives are in turmoil and their futures uncertain and they just seek to know why, and what they can do to know God. 

This is why the current welfare system is so cruel. It provides sustenance and comfort. It artificially created the same condition in them as the wealthy. They become wise in their own conceits. Sadly, this is all too common these days. 

There are few that are searching out wisdom. There aren't many in America where poverty hits hard enough to make a person stop counting on things and their own entitled arrogance, and be humbled. Praise God for fields of white, ready to be harvested in some areas.

<!-- post 17387 · 2026-04-12 07:56 · study -->

Moving on: When the righteous men rejoice, there is great glory. When God has blessed His people with leaders who openly praise His name, we have the opportunity for glory. But when the wicked men rise, the righteous are hidden. 

Tying this again to soul-winning, evangelizing to the lost, praising God's name in the open, or even reading the Bible and speaking His word confidently, is easy in modern America, today. We have the freedom to openly glorify God. Our leaders, even if they are wolves and deceivers, pay lip service to the Lord and believers are able to openly proclaim Christ without fear. 

Yet so many men are hidden, still. It's a shame that many churches do not have any soul-winning. That many Christians hide who they are, and what Jesus has done for them. That so few read the Bible here, while there are countries where reading scripture can be a prison sentence. That confessing Christ can end in death. 

Maybe it is a parallel to the rich young man. That by our comfort and safety we grow complacent. Maybe revival requires tribulation. Maybe the embarrassment of riches we have in the freedom we've been granted by God has caused too many Christians to have their heart hardened for the lost. 

Vs 13; *He that covereth his sins shall not prosper: but whoso confesseth and forsaketh them shall have mercy.*

We know that in Christ our sins are forgiven, past, present and future. But we are also to faithfully confess our sins to the Father, Who is faithful again to forgive. 

Covering sin leads to the chastening of the Lord. Humbling ourselves to admit our failings will have mercy. 

Keeping the theme going, you can see how those Christians who don't go to church, don't read their Bible, don't spread the gospel, would rather hide their sin than acknowledge and foresake them. If they confess, they're conceding to God that they know better, and if they agree that His way is the way forward, then they cannot continue on in theirs. 

They become paralyzed by their own guilty conscience and love of their sin. 

"If I go to church, I am admitting I should, and so now I am on the hook to keep going." Same with Bible reading, prayer, or evangelizing. 

We read in vs 9 that if you hate hearing the law because you want to continue doing the things God says you shouldn't, even your prayers shall be abominations.

How much worse are our prayers when we have the liberty to go to church, to get plugged in, to hear hard preaching, to learn God's word, to hear the law and confess our sins and be forgiven, and have the freedom to joyfully tell others the riches of God, and yet we ignore it all?

Is it any wonder we end up with a wicked ruler will rise up over a spiritually poor people? To take away the things and riches we take for granted? To provoke God's people to turn again to Him and do what's right, that He may heal the land? 

V 14-15 seem to be separate points, yet in this larger context they reveal a greater truth;

*Happy is the man that feareth alway: but he that hardeneth his heart shall fall into mischief. As a roaring lion, and a ranging bear; so is a wicked ruler over the poor people.*

Don't harden your hearts. Humble yourself. Confess your sins, including those that come from omissions. To know to do good and to do it not. Return to the first love. Obey the Great Commission. We have been given the ministry of reconciliation, and the opportunity to be servants to the Lord. Don't squander it. Redeem the time. Repent, ask forgiveness and turn to the paths of the Lord.

<!-- post 17391 · 2026-04-14 06:02 · study -->

Continuing the series on Proverbs chapter 28; *The prince that wanteth understanding is also a great oppressor: but he that hateth covetousness shall prolong his days*.

This saying ties together two seemingly disparate concepts, but as advice to a young prince of the kingdom, they tie together. Lack of understanding leads to covetousness. Not having knowledge and the wisdom to execute it, leads to a desire for things. As we've already discussed, regarding the rich young man and Jesus, want for understanding and desire for "things" go hand in hand. Longing for riches, seeking what's not yours, fills the hole that's left. 

Vs 17 warns to let the bloody man go to where his violence shall take him. To his own violent end. 

Vs 18-28 are another set of contrasting sayings, between the good man and wicked, but focusing on a particular type of behavior that is driven by seeking gain without the work it takes to earn it. And warnings against being corrupt enough to be taken in by vain men. 

Those who walk upright, honest in their dealings shall be saved. Not necessarily in the gospel sense, although that may apply, but in the "sudden destruction" that comes upon the wicked. The man perverse in his ways will see their life fall apart quickly. A corrupt and crooked politician, suddenly exposed as a degenerate and a whoremonger, for example. It is as inevitable as the bloody man's race to the pit. 

The prudent man, who tilleth his land, who tends to the fields now that they may harvest later, will have plenty. But those that follow after vain persons shall have poverty. 

The vain hope in flatterers, seducers and false teachers, perverse political leaders, will take those that follow them, by covetousness and sloth, itchy ears and lusts. But there is no profit in the energy expended on vain pursuit. 

*A faithful man shall abound with blessings: but he that maketh haste to be rich shall not be innocent.*..*To have respect if persons is not good: for for a piece of bread that man will transgress...He that hastens to be rich hath an evil eye, and consdereth not that poverty shall come upon him.*

The theme of covetousness and following after vain persons really hits hard in v20-22. The faithful man will abound with blessings, but those looking to get rich quick choose to corrupt themselves in their greed. 

The "Prosperity Gospel" scam artists take as much advantage of this tendency in man as the street corner swindler. The old secular saying, "you can't con an honest man" is simply resting this biblical truth. 

Having respect of persons, favoring one over others because of their wealth, their position, their power, is wrong, not only because it leads to treating the poor and powerless as less than yourself, but instills a form of covetousness in your heart that makes it easier for these false teachers, crept in unawares, to make merchandise of you. 

This tendency towards greedy covetousness and seeking to gain favor with those you favor for appearance and empty promises, will lead you to sell out for a piece of bread. For a small fraction of what you could have earned honestly with prudent diligence. 

He that hasteneth to be rich, is being guided by their evil eye, not by the ways of the Lord. 

**1 John 2:16**
*For all that is in the world, the lust of the flesh, and the lust of the eyes, and the pride of life, is not of the Father, but is of the world.*

This can strike anyone, at any age or station. Presidents and kings can be taken out by the same lusts peddled by vain men, as the poorest man. 

They delude themselves that their end won't be poverty, but as we've seen, the perverse in his ways shall fall all at once. And great is that fall. 

Better to be the upright man who rebukes those being taken in by the flattering tongue of a con man. Better to till your field for a future harvest. Better to do the work of the Lord for rewards in Heaven.

<!-- post 17392 · 2026-04-14 06:02 · study -->

Put your trust in the Lord, and you shall be made fat. Humble yourself before God, and don't let walking in your ways, pride in your heart that the flattering tongue can appeal to, the coveting of riches, the desire for clout and fame, ruin you, and cause divisions and strife from your pride. 

Walk wisely, not as a fool. Don't be a respecter of persons, don't value things over people. Don't worry about giving freely to the poor, you are leading to the Lord and He will repay. Don't turn a blind eye to the suffering that you can help alleviate. To know to do good and do it not is a sin. A curse. 

Follow after God. The wicked will perish and He will deliver us from tribulation. Bless us for our diligence and charity. Reward us for not seeking after riches, but seeking understanding. And when the wicked perish, the righteous increase. Their ill gotten gain will be gathered for him that pity the poor. God will see to it.

<!-- post 17394 · 2026-04-15 06:30 · study -->

**Proverbs 28:24** *Whoso robbeth his father or his mother, and saith, It is no transgression; the same is the companion of a destroyer.*

The last verse in this study of Proverbs 28 deals with a child who steals from their parents, excusing it as "no transgression," saying it is not a sin, the same is the companion of a destroyer. 

**Exodus 20:12**
*Honour thy father and thy mother: that thy days may be long upon the land which the Lord thy God giveth thee.*

As the fifth of the Ten Commandments, it emphasizes filial respect, obedience, and care, including financial support. It is notable as the first commandment with a direct promise of blessing;

**Ephesians 6:1-3**
*Children, obey your parents in the Lord: for this is right. Honour thy father and mother; which is the first commandment with promise; That it may be well with thee, and thou mayest live long on the earth*.

While honor generally means respect or value, it frequently denotes a "heavy" weight or value, which in the KJV context translates to tangible actions like financial offerings. We see this use of the word in the payment of elders in the church;

**1 Timothy 5:17**
*Let the elders that rule well be counted worthy of double honour, especially they who labour in the word and doctrine*

The financial aspect of 'honor' is clear;

**Romans 13:7**
*Render therefore to all their dues: tribute to whom tribute is due; custom to whom custom; fear to whom fear; honour to whom honour*.

By including it in the list of taxes and tributes we are called to obey, we see the idea is supporting those above us, in positions to be honored, including parents. And also, the Lord;

**Proverbs 3:9**
*Honour the Lord with thy substance, and with the firstfruits of all thine increase*

Masking tithes and offerings unto the Lord, paying honor to whomever honor is due is a command. And it absolutely extends to children taking care of their parents.

**1 Timothy 5:3-4** 
*Honour widows that are widows indeed. But if any widow have children or nephews, let them learn first to shew piety at home, and to requite their parents: for that is good and acceptable before God.*

<!-- post 17395 · 2026-04-15 06:55 · study -->

**Matthew 15:1-9**
*Then came to Jesus scribes and Pharisees, which were of Jerusalem, saying, Why do thy disciples transgress the tradition of the elders? for they wash not their hands when they eat bread. But he answered and said unto them, Why do ye also transgress the commandment of God by your tradition? For God commanded, saying, Honour thy father and mother: and, He that curseth father or mother, let him die the death. But ye say, Whosoever shall say to his father or his mother, It is a gift, by whatsoever thou mightest be profited by me; And honour not his father or his mother, he shall be free. Thus have ye made the commandment of God of none effect by your tradition. Ye hypocrites, well did Esaias prophesy of you, saying, This people draweth nigh unto me with their mouth, and honoureth me with their lips; but their heart is far from me. But in vain they do worship me, teaching for doctrines the commandments of men.*

Here Jesus reinforces the concept of honoring your father and mother, referencing the honor due them, and placing the command against cursing your parents as the direct counterpoint to the 5th Commandment. 

**Leviticus 20:9**
*For every one that curseth his father or his mother shall be surely put to death: he hath cursed his father or his mother; his blood shall be upon him.*

The promise of a long life upon the earth for those that honor their parents, versus the death that those who fail to, who rob their parents, deserve. 

The doctrine of the Pharisees held that a son who pledged his wealth to the temple, while yet holding on to it for his own use, could declare it as a gift, and exempt the person from their duty to their father and mother. 

**Mark 7:11**
*But ye say, If a man shall say to his father or mother, It is Corban, that is to say, a gift, by whatsoever thou mightest be profited by me; he shall be free.*

This commandment of men made God's law to no effect. Creating a holier than thou excuse. 

The curse of God upon a people who dishonor their parents, leading them to destruction, Is something that we have seen in modern times. 

The baby boomer generation was the first to abandon the care and support of their parents to the state. To nursing homes. To their health insurance and Medicare. Food stamps. They also abandoned care of their children to schools and day cares. They broke the generational cycle God had put into place and we have all suffered for it. 

They took the inheritance of their parents and squandered it while bragging that they're spending their grandchildren's inheritance on frivolous things. 

It's fitting that many have broken relationships with their now dead parents, and their children and grandchildren. And sadly, they spend their final days alone in the same nursing homes they consigned their own parents to. 

God put in place a system of family, of fathers in a hierarchy, wives in respectful submission to their husband, children under both, and honor due to the elders, to the widow indeed. A multi-generational family where children are arrows in the quiver of the father. Where each generation provides something to the unit, and stability to society. 

But those that have broken this tradition the same is a companion of the destroyer. They've brought only suffering and death.

<!-- post 17396 · 2026-04-15 07:08 · study -->

As always, Jesus is our example. 

**Philippians 2:5-8**
*Let this mind be in you, which was also in Christ Jesus: Who, being in the form of God, thought it not robbery to be equal with God: But made himself of no reputation, and took upon him the form of a servant, and was made in the likeness of men: And being found in fashion as a man, he humbled himself, and became obedient unto death, even the death of the cross.*

Being obedient unto death, honoring the Father. The good Son. 

**John 6:38**
*For I came down from heaven, not to do mine own will, but the will of him that sent me*.

**John 14:31**
*But that the world may know that I love the Father; and as the Father gave me commandment, even so I do...*

**John 15:10**
*If ye keep my commandments, ye shall abide in my love; even as I have kept my Father's commandments, and abide in his love*.

Jesus provided the example of a lifelong commitment to honoring the Father. Of doing His will. Of obeying His Commandments. A lesson in obedience that we are called to emulate. 

**Hebrews 5:8**
*Though he were a Son, yet learned he obedience by the things which he suffered*.

As children of God by faith, as heirs, we owe the same honor and respect and obedience to the Lord. Even obedience unto death. Which is why martyrs are honored in heaven. Receiving greater reward and crowns. 

Loving God with your whole whole and soul, having fear of the Lord, begins with fearing your parents. That is why there are so many Proverbs revolving around being a good son. Why so many call upon parents to raise their children properly. 

Obedient children are a reflection of Christ and good parents are a reflection of the Lord. God, our Father. The ordered society that comes from it is a blessing. The obedience that it be well with us, that we live long upon the Earth. And to better know Him. Amen

**Proverbs 28**
*The wicked flee when no man pursueth: but the righteous are bold as a lion.*

*2 For the transgression of a land many are the princes thereof: but by a man of understanding and knowledge the state thereof shall be prolonged.*

*3 A poor man that oppresseth the poor is like a sweeping rain which leaveth no food.*

*4 They that forsake the law praise the wicked: but such as keep the law contend with them.*

*5 Evil men understand not judgment: but they that seek the Lord understand all things.*

*6 Better is the poor that walketh in his uprightness, than he that is perverse in his ways, though he be rich.*

*7 Whoso keepeth the law is a wise son: but he that is a companion of riotous men shameth his father.*

*8 He that by usury and unjust gain increaseth his substance, he shall gather it for him that will pity the poor.*

*9 He that turneth away his ear from hearing the law, even his prayer shall be abomination.*

*10 Whoso causeth the righteous to go astray in an evil way, he shall fall himself into his own pit: but the upright shall have good things in possession.*

*11 The rich man is wise in his own conceit; but the poor that hath understanding searcheth him out.*

*12 When righteous men do rejoice, there is great glory: but when the wicked rise, a man is hidden.*

*13 He that covereth his sins shall not prosper: but whoso confesseth and forsaketh them shall have mercy.*

*14 Happy is the man that feareth alway: but he that hardeneth his heart shall fall into mischief.*

*15 As a roaring lion, and a ranging bear; so is a wicked ruler over the poor people.*

*16 The prince that wanteth understanding is also a great oppressor: but he that hateth covetousness shall prolong his days.*

*17 A man that doeth violence to the blood of any person shall flee to the pit; let no man stay him.*

*18 Whoso walketh uprightly shall be saved: but he that is perverse in his ways shall fall at once.*

*19 He that tilleth his land shall have plenty of bread: but he that followeth after vain persons shall have poverty enough.*

*20 A faithful man shall abound with blessings: but he that maketh haste to be rich shall not be innocent.*

*21 To have respect of persons is not good: for for a piece of bread that man will transgress.*

*22 He that hasteth to be rich hath an evil eye, and considereth not that poverty shall come upon him.*

*23 He that rebuketh a man afterwards shall find more favour than he that flattereth with the tongue.*

*24 Whoso robbeth his father or his mother, and saith, It is no transgression; the same is the companion of a destroyer.*

*25 He that is of a proud heart stirreth up strife: but he that putteth his trust in the Lord shall be made fat.*

*26 He that trusteth in his own heart is a fool: but whoso walketh wisely, he shall be delivered.*

*27 He that giveth unto the poor shall not lack: but he that hideth his eyes shall have many a curse.*

*28 When the wicked rise, men hide themselves: but when they perish, the righteous increase.*

## Proverbs 29  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-04-16

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17397

<!-- post 17397 · 2026-04-16 05:40 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 29**
*He, that being often reproved hardeneth his neck, shall suddenly be destroyed, and that without remedy.*

*2 When the righteous are in authority, the people rejoice: but when the wicked beareth rule, the people mourn.*

*3 Whoso loveth wisdom rejoiceth his father: but he that keepeth company with harlots spendeth his substance.*

*4 The king by judgment establisheth the land: but he that receiveth gifts overthroweth it.*

*5 A man that flattereth his neighbour spreadeth a net for his feet.*

*6 In the transgression of an evil man there is a snare: but the righteous doth sing and rejoice.*

*7 The righteous considereth the cause of the poor: but the wicked regardeth not to know it.*

*8 Scornful men bring a city into a snare: but wise men turn away wrath.*

*9 If a wise man contendeth with a foolish man, whether he rage or laugh, there is no rest.*

*10 The bloodthirsty hate the upright: but the just seek his soul.*

*11 A fool uttereth all his mind: but a wise man keepeth it in till afterwards.*

*12 If a ruler hearken to lies, all his servants are wicked.*

*13 The poor and the deceitful man meet together: the Lord lighteneth both their eyes.*

*14 The king that faithfully judgeth the poor, his throne shall be established for ever.*

*15 The rod and reproof give wisdom: but a child left to himself bringeth his mother to shame.*

*16 When the wicked are multiplied, transgression increaseth: but the righteous shall see their fall.*

*17 Correct thy son, and he shall give thee rest; yea, he shall give delight unto thy soul.*

*18 Where there is no vision, the people perish: but he that keepeth the law, happy is he.*

*19 A servant will not be corrected by words: for though he understand he will not answer.*

*20 Seest thou a man that is hasty in his words? there is more hope of a fool than of him.*

*21 He that delicately bringeth up his servant from a child shall have him become his son at the length.*

*22 An angry man stirreth up strife, and a furious man aboundeth in transgression.*

*23 A man's pride shall bring him low: but honour shall uphold the humble in spirit.*

*24 Whoso is partner with a thief hateth his own soul: he heareth cursing, and bewrayeth it not.*

*25 The fear of man bringeth a snare: but whoso putteth his trust in the Lord shall be safe.*

*26 Many seek the ruler's favour; but every man's judgment cometh from the Lord.*

*27 An unjust man is an abomination to the just: and he that is upright in the way is abomination to the wicked.*

<!-- post 17398 · 2026-04-16 06:05 · study -->

**Proverbs 29**, the last of the sayings of Solomon transcribed by order of Hezekiah, begins with the reality of reprobation in those that stiffen their necks to reproof. The Bible often shows examples of heart hardened people, like Pharaoh and the Pharisees, coming to destruction without remedy, despite the reproof of the Lord. 

**Romans 1:18-19**
*For the wrath of God is revealed from heaven against all ungodliness and unrighteousness of men, who hold the truth in unrighteousness; Because that which may be known of God is manifest in them; for God hath shewed it unto them.*

Plagues, curses from Heaven, the reproof of Christ among them, all revealing the iniquity and unrighteousness of men, who refused to acknowledge the truth set before them. And their foolish hearts darkened. There became no more remedy for them, no repentance towards the truth of God. And so they are destroyed. 

Vs 2 echos similar sayings, that the rule of the righteous brings joy to the people, but the wicked kings bring mourning. 

Vs 3 brings another reminder to honor your father. A wise son will pay tribute to him, but the whoremongers spend what should rightfully be the family's wealth. 

Verse 4-8 show some of the ways that a wicked man lays snares for kings and neighbors. With gifts and flattering words, seeking favor. Or by the transgression of an evil man, there lies a snare of sin for others. Vengeance, wrath, or even common cause for filthy lucer's sake, others can be caught up in it when the wicked sin. But the righteous song and rejoice, happily trusting God. Avoiding those pitfalls. 

The righteous consider the poor, and turn away wrath. But the wicked don't care. Their scorn for the afflicted and bring others to transgression. 

Vs *10 The bloodthirsty hate the upright: but the just seek his soul.* 

The bloodthirsty, the violent man, the scorners and scoffers and fools, they all hate the upright man, the righteous in Christ. Their deeds are wicked, and the good man magnifies the light within him. 

**John 3:20**
*For every one that doeth evil hateth the light, neither cometh to the light, lest his deeds should be reproved*.

Jesus warns us of this truth;

**John 15:18-19**
*If the world hate you, ye know that it hated me before it hated you. If ye were of the world, the world would love his own: but because ye are not of the world, but I have chosen you out of the world, therefore the world hateth you.*

They hate His children because they hate Him. They hate the light that exposes their evil. They hate reproof and become stiff necked. They hold the truth in unrighteousness.

<!-- post 17403 · 2026-04-17 05:57 · study -->

Continuing in **Proverbs 29:11**

*A fool uttereth all his mind: but a wise man keepeth it in till afterwards*.

Keeping your tongue, holding back your opinions and thoughts, is a measure of wisdom. Discerned speech, as we have seen repeated in Proverbs, is valuable. Blabbing every idle thought that comes into your head is vain. 

Rulers who believe lies, he will be surrounded by wicked servants. This could have dual meaning. Either, due to his own delusion and desire to be told lies, only the wicked prosper in his court. Or, if he is acting on lies, the servants carrying out his will are made wicked. Both are true. 

Vs 13 is another reminder that we all, rich or poor, good or wicked, are given life by the same Lord. Another way of expressing the opposite end of life found in **Ecclesiastes**;

**Ecclesiastes 2:14**
*The wise man's eyes are in his head, but the fool walks in darkness: and I myself perceived also that one event happeneth to them all.* 

**Ecclesiastes 9:2**
*All things come alike to all: there is one event to the righteous, and to the wicked; to the good and to the clean, and to the unclean; to him that sacrificeth, and to him that sacrificeth not: as is the good, so is the sinner; and he that sweareth, as he that feareth an oath.*

If all men are alive, their eyes lightened by the Lord, and all men die, having the same end, then all the things we gather, the vows we make, the wood, hay and stubble we accumulate, all is vanity. We should have no respect of persons and we should all seek that which is eternal, in Christ. This is the way. 

*The king that faithfully judgeth the poor, his throne shall be established for ever.*

There is truth in the saying, that a good king or ruler will judge the poor fairly and righteously, and prosper. But only one King has faithfully judged all men, who are all poor in His sight, and His throne is established forever. Christ Jesus. 

Vs 15 is another admonition to not spare the rod of correction for a child. Feral children, left to their own foolishness, undisciplined, will bring their mother to shame. 

Vs 17 is a companion to 15, *Correct thy son, and he shall give thee rest; yea, he shall give delight unto thy soul.*

Sandwiched in between these sayings, vs 16 says; *When the wicked are multiplied, transgression increaseth: but the righteous shall see their fall.*

The fastest way of multiplying the wicked is to raise a generation without fear of their parents or fear for the Lord. They lack wisdom, fear of the Lord is its foundation. They shame their parents. They indulge themselves and think not of others. The city is made bloody and lacks defenses against the stranger.

But vs 16 also is prophetic. 

**2 Timothy 3:1-7**
*This know also, that in the last days perilous times shall come. For men shall be lovers of their own selves, covetous, boasters, proud, blasphemers, disobedient to parents, unthankful, unholy, Without natural affection, trucebreakers, false accusers, incontinent, fierce, despisers of those that are good, Traitors, heady, highminded, lovers of pleasures more than lovers of God; Having a form of godliness, but denying the power thereof: from such turn away. For of this sort are they which creep into houses, and lead captive silly women laden with sins, led away with divers lusts, Ever learning, and never able to come to the knowledge of the truth.*

The wicked will be multiplied, but the righteous shall see their fall. In the end times, we who have overcome in Christ will see the wrath of God poured out on them. 

Where there is no vision, no righteousness of a king that follows the law, where there is no understanding of the ways of the Lord, the promises of God though His commandments, no prophecy and appreciation for the end times promise and eternal rewards, the people will perish.

<!-- post 17405 · 2026-04-19 05:55 · study -->

Finishing up Proverbs 29.

V19 A servant will not be corrected by words: for though he understand he will not answer. 

A master verbally correcting a servant has to weigh the fact that they may understand, but cannot answer for themselves due to fear. 

Don't be hasty with your words. Again, the discernment and temperance to withhold your words, rather than rushing to speak is wisdom. 

Those entrusted to our stewardship young, become as sons. 

More familiar sayings. Angry men stir up strife. Pride brings man low. Honor upholds the humble. 

Many of the repeated sayings remind me of those things my parents often said. "I told you a million times!" Repetition brings understanding. God wants us to internalize these things. 

My Father often said "Control yourself, or someone else will and you won't like it much." My mom, "you are your friends." These often said sayings stuck. And I passed them down with a few of my own. 

Don't keep company with a thief or you'll regret it. 

V*25 The fear of man bringeth a snare: but whoso putteth his trust in the Lord shall be safe*. Is yet another gospel message. We have security in the Lord when we trust in Him alone. So don't be afraid. 

Seek the favor of the Lord, from Whom all judgment comes, the rest will fall into place. 

V27 *An unjust man is an abomination to the just: and he that is upright in the way is abomination to the wicked.*

The children of disobedience are in conflict with the children of God, and vice versa. Don't be surprised when they hate you for His sake.

*4 The king by judgment establisheth the land: but he that receiveth gifts overthroweth it.*

*5 A man that flattereth his neighbour spreadeth a net for his feet.*

*6 In the transgression of an evil man there is a snare: but the righteous doth sing and rejoice.*

*7 The righteous considereth the cause of the poor: but the wicked regardeth not to know it.*

*8 Scornful men bring a city into a snare: but wise men turn away wrath.*

*9 If a wise man contendeth with a foolish man, whether he rage or laugh, there is no rest.*

*10 The bloodthirsty hate the upright: but the just seek his soul.*

*11 A fool uttereth all his mind: but a wise man keepeth it in till afterwards.*

*12 If a ruler hearken to lies, all his servants are wicked.*

*13 The poor and the deceitful man meet together: the Lord lighteneth both their eyes.*

*14 The king that faithfully judgeth the poor, his throne shall be established for ever.*

*15 The rod and reproof give wisdom: but a child left to himself bringeth his mother to shame.*

*16 When the wicked are multiplied, transgression increaseth: but the righteous shall see their fall.*

*17 Correct thy son, and he shall give thee rest; yea, he shall give delight unto thy soul.*

*18 Where there is no vision, the people perish: but he that keepeth the law, happy is he.*

*19 A servant will not be corrected by words: for though he understand he will not answer.*

*20 Seest thou a man that is hasty in his words? there is more hope of a fool than of him.*

*21 He that delicately bringeth up his servant from a child shall have him become his son at the length.*

*22 An angry man stirreth up strife, and a furious man aboundeth in transgression.*

*23 A man's pride shall bring him low: but honour shall uphold the humble in spirit.*

*24 Whoso is partner with a thief hateth his own soul: he heareth cursing, and bewrayeth it not.*

<!-- post 17410 · 2026-04-20 07:03 · study -->

**Proverbs 30** introduces the "sayings of Agur," who, some argue, is not Solomon, but possibly the sayings of a Gentile wiseman, under the influence of the Spirit. However, Jewish tradition views this as a pseudonym for King Solomon, describing him as one who "gathered" or collected wisdom. 

Jakeh, means pious and faithfully religious. It may be a title, rather than a name. 

The passage is addressed to Ithiel and Ucal, possibly students or symbolic names meaning "God with me" and "I am strong," respectively. 

These wise sayings were included in the scripture that Hezekiah's scribes compiled at the righteous King's request. 

The humble self-deprecation of the introduction in v2-3 ironically demonstrates this man's wisdom. Admission that as a brutish, or generally stupid man, with no training or knowledge, before dropping insightful and prophetic oracles, is a key to wisdom. Humility and willingness to grasp that even the smartest man doesn't know all the ways of God, is itself wise. 

Immediately, we are given an understanding of God and prophetic mention of Jesus, that also serves as a reminder of the limitations man has in understanding God. 

Asking rhetorically, Who it is that has done these things? What is His name? We know it is the Lord. What is His Son's name? We know it is Jesus. But the glass darkly that they saw through pointed only at the Son, not the name. Further revelation has given us the only name under Heaven by which a man may be saved.

Regardless of the identity of the human author, the Spirit is showing us that even in the day of Solomon, and the Kings of Judah, God's Christ was known and believed on. 

v5-6 give the inspired case for Biblical fundamentalism. Every word of God is pure. 

**2 Peter 1:19-21**
*We have also a more sure word of prophecy; whereunto ye do well that ye take heed, as unto a light that shineth in a dark place, until the day dawn, and the day star arise in your hearts: Knowing this first, that no prophecy of the scripture is of any private interpretation. For the prophecy came not in old time by the will of man: but holy men of God spake as they were moved by the Holy Ghost.*

And if every word of God is pure, we need an "every word" Bible. We are blessed that we have one, in the Authorized version, in English. Preserved by God unto all generations.

*God is a shield unto them that out their trust in Him* is the promise of the gospel. The same yesterday, today and forever. 

And the curse against changing His word, adding to it, bringing false gospels, remains consistent;

*Add thou not unto his words, lest he reprove thee, and thou be found a liar*.

Be it Satan, in the beginning, asking Eve "Yea, hath God said" in **Genesis 3:1,** or modern churches claiming Christ while adding to and perverting the word to deceive. The wrath of God against those that corrupt His pure word.

**Deuteronomy 4:2**
*Ye shall not add unto the word which I command you, neither shall ye diminish ought from it, that ye may keep the commandments of the Lord your God which I command you.*

**Revelation 22:18-19**
*For I testify unto every man that heareth the words of the prophecy of this book, If any man shall add unto these things, God shall add unto him the plagues that are written in this book: And if any man shall take away from the words of the book of this prophecy, God shall take away his part out of the book of life, and out of the holy city, and from the things which are written in this book.*

The sure knowledge that we can trust the word of God is a foundation which we can build a strong fortress upon. 

**Matthew 7:24-25**
*Therefore whosoever heareth these sayings of mine, and doeth them, I will liken him unto a wise man, which built his house upon a rock: And the rain descended, and the floods came, and the winds blew, and beat upon that house; and it fell not: for it was founded upon a rock.*

<!-- post 17412 · 2026-04-21 06:48 · study -->

Continuing **Proverbs 30** and the sayings attributed to Agur the son of Jakeh. 

Vs 7-9 is a form of prayer to the Lord to grant Agur favor in this lifetime; That God removes from him vanity and lies and to give neither poverty or riches, yet have sufficient food to eat. 

*Lest I be full, and deny thee, and say, Who is the Lord? or lest I be poor, and steal, and take the name of my God in vain.*

This should be the attitude we strive for. To take away the worthless and useless things in our life that take us away from fully serving God. That we deal honestly, not only with others but ourselves and therefore with God. Hiding nothing. Rationalizing or excusing nothing. Not heeding delusion that covers sin. 

Being satisfied with what God has blessed you with. Being content to have sufficient food to eat. Living simply unto the Lord. As many Proverbs before this have shown, the rich place their trust in their possessions. Themselves. They deny God and say "Who is the Lord?" 

Make no mistake, that even the temporary and sudden wealth an average or lower middle-class person may receive, a windfall or an unexpected inheritance or bonus, is often sufficient to knock them off the pathway of God. To make them forget the blessings and the Blesser. How much worse to have the wealth always about you, where you want for nothing and grow complacent towards God?

And be thankful that you're not poor, and left to steal to eat. To curse God for your station and take His name in vain. 

Sometimes, in our Christian walk, we are tempted. Covetousness turns to greed. How often do we pray, not with thanksgiving for what we have, but desiring "just a little more, God, please?" That He would increase our wealth so we can afford some luxury or extravagance that we've convinced ourselves that we deserve. "Everyone else has one!" Coveting. "If we only had a little more!" Greed. 

**Philippians 4:12**
*I know both how to be abased, and I know how to abound: every where and in all things I am instructed both to be full and to be hungry, both to abound and to suffer need.*

Paul writes the end goal that Agur sought. To know how to be both rich and poor, everywhere in all things, to know *whatsoever state I am, therewith to be content.*

Why? *I can do all things through Christ which strengtheneth me.*

The vanity of riches and the lies that rationalize sin away, are traps we should call upon the Lord to save us from. Contentment and gratitude go a long way to remove these things far from us.

<!-- post 17413 · 2026-04-21 07:00 · study -->

An example of this in my life, a small thing that maybe others can relate to; After grocery shopping, putting away the perishables in the fridge, and there being no room for the plenty, and cursing how nothing more fits, and finding old leftovers and nearly empty bottles of sauces, angrily throwing them away to make room for more...Or the pantry too full. Cans or boxes falling out because the shelf is packed, and cursing to yourself, "Dang it!"

Imagine having so much plenty that this is a problem, and then asking God separately, "Lord, could you please increase my salary?" 

Or worse, many keep subscriptions to worldly streaming services, eat out multiple times a week, blow their money on a video game or some fancy brand clothing, or on bad and sinful habits like drinking, smoking or gambling...AND then asking God to give you more! 

We all do it to some degree, and you don't have to be on the extreme ends of rich or poor to have vanity and lies in our life. To forget the Lord, and deny Him. To justify sin and use His name in vain. 

Gratitude for the grace of God should be sufficient for us. Agur's prayer should be our own. Like Paul we need to work on being content, and doing the work of the Lord, allowing Him to bless us and provide what we need, keeping the vanity and lies away from us, leading us not into temptation. Delivering us from evil, even the evil of our own heart.

<!-- post 17418 · 2026-04-23 06:26 · study -->

Continuing **Proverbs 30:10** 
*Accuse not a servant unto his master, lest he curse thee, and thou be found guilty.*

The practical application of this is to guard against accusing the master through his proxy, his servant, especially false or exaggerated claims, that expose hypocrisy and deficiency in your own life. An accusation against another man's servant is an accusation against the master's stewardship. If the servant did wrong, dealt dishonestly, was slothful, the implication is that the master can't oversee his own, or is lax in some way. 

Spiritually, Paul uses this proverb to warn against brothers in Christ accusing one another for their failings in their Christian walk. 

**Romans 14:4**
*Who art thou that judgest another man's servant? to his own master he standeth or falleth. Yea, he shall be holden up: for God is able to make him stand.*

We are answerable to God. If one is falling down in some area, weak in the faith, God will correct them. Accusing them accuses Him, and opens yourself up to being called out and chastened for your own deficiency before God. 

It isn't an excuse to enable, and standards and church discipline still exist. Fellowship can still be broken. But encouraging speech, edifying one another with fear and admonition of the Lord, is what we should use. Not accusations of being less holy than thou. Meekness and humility, with the grace to understand you're not perfect either, is the key. 

As Christ taught;

**Matthew 7:3-5**
*And why beholdest thou the mote that is in thy brother's eye, but considerest not the beam that is in thine own eye? Or how wilt thou say to thy brother, Let me pull out the mote out of thine eye; and, behold, a beam is in thine own eye? Thou hypocrite, first cast out the beam out of thine own eye; and then shalt thou see clearly to cast out the mote out of thy brother's eye.*

So, we must be cautious that we aren't projecting, that we who also serve the Lord aren't falling down in our own way. To not be a hypocrite, least He curse us and we be found guilty.

<!-- post 17419 · 2026-04-23 07:07 · study -->

**v11-14** contains an indictment of a group of people, a crooked and perverse generation;

*There is a generation that curseth their father, and doth not bless their mother. There is a generation that are pure in their own eyes, and yet is not washed from their filthiness. There is a generation, O how lofty are their eyes! and their eyelids are lifted up. There is a generation, whose teeth are as swords, and their jaw teeth as knives, to devour the poor from off the earth, and the needy from among men*.

Many are always quick to apply this to "kids today," or to Baby Boomers, and arguments can be made to support this, but it's a mistake to isolate this as purely prophetic of specific cohorts united only by age, and attribute to them all these evils. 

The generation is always with us. There IS a generation like this, the Bible says. There are always evil people, who curse their father and do not bless their mother. Who reject the instruction of their parents and do not honor them. Disobedient to parents. 

There are always segments of the population who are pure in their own eyes, justifying the filth of their flesh and the lust in their hearts. 

There are always prideful and arrogant people, who see themselves as above the righteous, while they live in the gutter. Who reject the ways of the Lord, to walk the paths of folly. 

People who use words as weapons, inventors of evil, who use and exploitation and extortion, usury and inequity to destroy others. 

There are always reprobates among us. 

**Romans 1:29-32** 
*Being filled with all unrighteousness, fornication, wickedness, covetousness, maliciousness; full of envy, murder, debate, deceit, malignity; whisperers, Backbiters, haters of God, despiteful, proud, boasters, inventors of evil things, disobedient to parents, Without understanding, covenantbreakers, without natural affection, implacable, unmerciful: Who knowing the judgment of God, that they which commit such things are worthy of death, not only do the same, but have pleasure in them that do them.*

<!-- post 17427 · 2026-04-24 07:33 · study -->

**Proverbs 30:15-16**
*The horseleach hath two daughters, crying, Give, give. There are three things that are never satisfied, yea, four things say not, It is enough:The grave; and the barren womb; the earth that is not filled with water; and the fire that saith not, It is enough.*

The proverb compares a bloodsucking leach, whose daughters cry "give, give," never satisfied, implacable, always wanting more, to four things in life; the grave (death), a barren womb, dry earth, and consuming fire. 

Death is inevitable, and decay and entropy the nature of the fallen world. There's always more graves needed, as there is always death. A barren womb is filled with the desperation of the woman, and never satisfied. Waters flood the dry earth, and still it will absorb it, and not be full. And fire cannot be quenched so long as there's something to consume. 

V17 *The eye that mocketh at his father, and despiseth to obey his mother, the ravens of the valley shall pick it out, and the young eagles shall eat it.* 

This curse against disobedient and dishonorable children shows the spiritual blindness that comes inevitably to those that will not honor their parents.

v18-19 Agur compares the seemingly random ways of an eagle soaring in the winds, a serpent moving upon a rock, and a ship cast to and fro on the sea to the understanding of a man with a woman. A beautiful picture of the ebbs and flows, the listing and sway of nature, to the romance and subtle grace of a man with a maid. 

Verse 20 tells us of the false justification that adulterous women delude themselves with. As someone cleans their mouth from eating, they think they can dismiss their sin with a hand wave. Rationalizing it away. 

v21-23 presents another "3 things/even 4" formula, this list of things that the earth is disquieted by, things that just aren't right; a servant put in charge, a fool who is filled with meat, an odious woman when she is married, and a handmaid that is heir to her mistress. 

The general idea is that all four lead to the person to think more highly of themselves than they ought to. It gives a false confidence to them that is troublesome to others. A servant put in a position of power they haven't earned, a maid given the inheritance of the household she served, a full fool who thinks he deserved the bounty he's received (and that it will continue), and an odious, obnoxious woman who has managed to find a husband just as she is. 

v24-28, another list of four. This time observation of animals and the wisdom that they provide; despite their relative weakness to man, ants still prepare for winter, storing their food in the summer. Foresight and patience, diligence and delaying gratification. 

The conies, the Syrian hyrax, a small, rabbit-like mammal found in Africa and the Middle East, often called rock badgers, are small, defenseless but place their dens in the rock of the mountainside. They wisely seek shelter that protects them. (There are spiritual applications to this as well, both the believer taking refuge in the Rock, Christ Jesus, and the sinners asking the rocks to fall on them to try to avoid the wrath of God).

The locust swarm moves together as an army, without any king directing them. They work together. Cooperative achievement. 

The tiny spider weaving its way into palaces, a reminder that diligence can bring even the weak to places of strength. 

v29-31, yet another list of 3/4; the opposite of v21-23, which were disturbing things. This list are pleasant to the observer.

A strong lion that fears no one, a grayhound's speed, and a goats agility, are all like a king who is secure in his throne. Untouchable. 

v32 reminds us of we've done foolishly, lifting ourselves up in undeserved and even embarrassing shows of pride, or has thoughts of evil, intrusive or perhaps something we've ruminated in but caught ourselves, lay your hand over your mouth. Be silent and humble yourself. Don't dig the hole deeper with words that betray your foolish thoughts.

<!-- post 17428 · 2026-04-24 07:33 · study -->

Finally, v33 warns against anger leading to strife. Stirring up milk to butter, or a punch in the nose brings blood, forcing your wrath will cause conflict.

<!-- post 15627 · 2025-02-02 06:47 · study -->

The change in authorship in chapter 30 of Proverbs., from Solomon to Agur, is noticeable, yet the wisdom, and especially the prophecy, really stands out. God inspired, and deserving. 

When you read other books of "wisdom" from ancient philosophers to modern "self-help gurus" and then compare it to what is written here, it cannot stand up to God's eternal truth. 

*Every word of God is pure: he is a shield unto them that put their trust in him.*

One more chapter to go in **Proverbs**.
