## Proverbs 31  ·  Primary study  ·  2026-04-26

Source: https://t.me/HouseOfWews2/17429

<!-- post 17429 · 2026-04-26 06:46 · scripture -->

**Proverbs 31**
*The words of king Lemuel, the prophecy that his mother taught him.*

*2 What, my son? and what, the son of my womb? and what, the son of my vows?*

*3 Give not thy strength unto women, nor thy ways to that which destroyeth kings.*

*4 It is not for kings, O Lemuel, it is not for kings to drink wine; nor for princes strong drink:*

*5 Lest they drink, and forget the law, and pervert the judgment of any of the afflicted.*

*6 Give strong drink unto him that is ready to perish, and wine unto those that be of heavy hearts.*

*7 Let him drink, and forget his poverty, and remember his misery no more.*

*8 Open thy mouth for the dumb in the cause of all such as are appointed to destruction.*

*9 Open thy mouth, judge righteously, and plead the cause of the poor and needy.*

*10 Who can find a virtuous woman? for her price is far above rubies.*

*11 The heart of her husband doth safely trust in her, so that he shall have no need of spoil.*

*12 She will do him good and not evil all the days of her life.*

*13 She seeketh wool, and flax, and worketh willingly with her hands.*

*14 She is like the merchants' ships; she bringeth her food from afar.*

*15 She riseth also while it is yet night, and giveth meat to her household, and a portion to her maidens.*

*16 She considereth a field, and buyeth it: with the fruit of her hands she planteth a vineyard.*

*17 She girdeth her loins with strength, and strengtheneth her arms.*

*18 She perceiveth that her merchandise is good: her candle goeth not out by night.*

*19 She layeth her hands to the spindle, and her hands hold the distaff.*

*20 She stretcheth out her hand to the poor; yea, she reacheth forth her hands to the needy.*

*21 She is not afraid of the snow for her household: for all her household are clothed with scarlet.*

*22 She maketh herself coverings of tapestry; her clothing is silk and purple.*

*23 Her husband is known in the gates, when he sitteth among the elders of the land.*

*24 She maketh fine linen, and selleth it; and delivereth girdles unto the merchant.*

*25 Strength and honour are her clothing; and she shall rejoice in time to come.*

*26 She openeth her mouth with wisdom; and in her tongue is the law of kindness.*

*27 She looketh well to the ways of her household, and eateth not the bread of idleness.*

*28 Her children arise up, and call her blessed; her husband also, and he praiseth her.*

*29 Many daughters have done virtuously, but thou excellest them all.*

*30 Favour is deceitful, and beauty is vain: but a woman that feareth the Lord, she shall be praised.*

*31 Give her of the fruit of her hands; and let her own works praise her in the gates.*

<!-- post 17430 · 2026-04-26 07:10 · study -->

The final chapter of **Proverbs**, attributed to the mother of King Lemuel, which is considered a pseudonym or pet name for King Solomon (meaning "devoted to God"), and possibly what Bathsheba called her son. This wisdom is a mother's, and as impactful as any given by a father. As we know, we should not forsake the law, or instruction of our parents. 

**Proverbs 1:8-9**
*My son, hear the instruction of thy father, and forsake not the law of thy mother: For they shall be an ornament of grace unto thy head, and chains about thy neck*.

And so here, we are privy to some of the sayings given a king by his mother. It begins with the loving devotion a mother has for her son, born of her womb, the fruit of her vows. A special relationship that fathers are blessed to witness and to share with their own mother, unique in the bond shared. 

Regardless of her identity, or his, we have the Spirit inspired wisdom given to us. 

*Give not thy strength unto women, nor thy ways to that which destroyeth kings*.

Women and being given to many wives were not just problems Solomon carried, but they were a downfall for his father David, as well. 

King David had eight named wives and an unspecified number of additional wives and concubines taken after he moved from Hebron to Jerusalem. 

Mical, his first, King Saul's daughter, who became bitter against him and had no children by him. 

Maacah, daughter of Talmai, king of Geshur, mother of Absalom, the son who rebelled against David, and of their daughter, Tamar. 

Ahinoam, the Jezreelitess, mother of Amnon, whose rape of his half-sister Tamar led Absalom to be bitter against David. 

During the rebellion of Absalom, he "*went in unto his father's concubines in the sight of all Israel*" on the roof of the palace, using them to humiliate David, and to make his claim to the throne through them. 

David's lust for Bathsheba, which led to the murder of Uriah the Hittite, her husband, the death of David's first son with her, as punishment from the Lord. 

All these things, and more, made David a bloody man, whose life was filled with strife and death. Thus, despite his desire to build the temple of God in Jerusalem, he was told he would not be allowed to;

**1 Chronicles 22:8** 
*But the word of the Lord came to me, saying, Thou hast shed blood abundantly, and hast made great wars: thou shalt not build an house unto my name, because thou hast shed much blood upon the earth in my sight.*

Bathsheba, having been witness to all these things, may well have imparted this wisdom of experience to her Lemuel. To her second son and living heir of David, in hopes that he would not foresake the law of his mother, and suffer the same as her husband. 

But in the end, we all fall short of the glory of God. Solomon was given the blessing of God to build the temple, which David had provisioned for. But his heart was not always right with God, for he gave his strength to women, and his ways to that which destroyeth kings.

<!-- post 17431 · 2026-04-27 05:39 · study -->

Continuing; Of the things that destroyeth kings, given as the law of a mother to her son; Alcohol. 

*It is not for kings, O Lemuel, it is not for kings to drink wine; nor for princes strong drink.*

This should be the end of the matter for saved believers. We are princes and heirs, co-heirs with Christ. We rule in the matters God has given us in this life, we are to rule the world with Him in the next. It is not for us to drink wine or strong drink. 

The only allowance for drinking alcohol is medicinally, for the perishing. For the dying. And for those with no hope, for the miserable. 

But we have the hope of Jesus. We are poor in spirit, and content with whatever state we are in. Those without, who don't know Christ, they drink to forget. 

God's word warns us that forgetfulness that comes from a drunken stupor leads to forgetting the law and perverting judgment. It is unbecoming of the princes of God to drink. We've been instructed to not even look upon it;

**Proverbs 23:31-33**
*Look not thou upon the wine when it is red, when it giveth his colour in the cup, when it moveth itself aright. At the last it biteth like a serpent, and stingeth like an adder. Thine eyes shall behold strange women, and thine heart shall utter perverse things.*

Without getting into the cope many professing Christians use to justify their drinking; that Jesus led the wedding party in Canna to drunkenness, giving them that had already been well-drunk to further drunkenness. Or that He used alcoholic wine at the last supper, rather than juice of the vine, new, as Jesus describes, or the various means of preparation and preservation used in ancient times to keep unfermented and low-fermented juice, watered down for consumption; the plain meaning of the text is undeniable. 

We may have liberty in Christ, and all things may be lawful, but all things do not edify. All things are not convenient. 

We should strive to be men of God, blameless. 

**2 Timothy 3:2-4** 
*A bishop then must be blameless, the husband of one wife, vigilant, sober, of good behaviour, given to hospitality, apt to teach; Not given to wine, no striker, not greedy of filthy lucre; but patient, not a brawler, not covetous; One that ruleth well his own house, having his children in subjection with all gravity*.

These standards for the pastors of a church exist to be the example for all young men. And women as well, *Even so must their wives be grave, not slanderers, sober, faithful in all things*.

*This is a true saying, if a man desire the office of a bishop, he desireth a good work.* We should all desire to be overseers and stewards of God's ministry of reconciliation that He has given us. Princes of the Kingdom. 

That means not being inebriated and forgetting the law. Not being led away by strange women in our alcoholic haze. Not uttering perverse things. Certainly not profaning Christ Jesus to justify our sinful habits.

<!-- post 17432 · 2026-04-27 05:52 · study -->

**Vs 8-9** *Open thy mouth for the dumb in the cause of all such as are appointed to destruction. Open thy mouth, judge righteously, and plead the cause of the poor and needy.*

The call to be a voice for the condemned without a voice, for the poor and needy, is the way of Kings and pleasing to God. 

Jesus, appearing before the judgment spoke not at all. 

**Matthew 27:12-14**
*And when he was accused of the chief priests and elders, he answered nothing. Then said Pilate unto him, Hearest thou not how many things they witness against thee? And he answered him to never a word; insomuch that the governor marvelled greatly.*

Jesus, appointed for destruction and mute to give His cause, directly exemplifies this saying. While Pilate could be excused for not knowing the proverbs (or the prophecy; 
**Isaiah 53:7** *He was oppressed, and he was afflicted, yet he opened not his mouth: he is brought as a lamb to the slaughter, and as a sheep before her shearers is dumb, so he openeth not his mouth.*) Herod, King of the Jews, and the presits and scribes should have. 

**Luke 23:8-9**
*And when Herod saw Jesus, he was exceeding glad: for he was desirous to see him of a long season, because he had heard many things of him; and he hoped to have seen some miracle done by him. Then he questioned with him in many words; but he answered him nothing.*

A fit ruler should speak for the condemned, to ensure justice. To consider the judgment. To plead the cause of those brought before them by the powerful, to ensure the poor and powerless aren't being falsely accused. 

The knowledge given to Lemuel by his mother was certainly heard by those princes of Judea, by their King, and ignored. What should have convicted them in the heart, witnessing Jesus, silent before them, instead was an indictment, that they had no excuse.

<!-- post 17433 · 2026-04-27 07:14 · study -->

One thing that we see again and again in Proverbs is a call for God's justice. Righteous judgment. Equity for the poor and oppressed. 

These are Christian virtues and values. But when a Christian Nation abandons them, when they hold vengeance and disdain for the poor as respecters of persons, then these ideas are hijacked by those seeking to defend the guilty. They are used as rhetorical cover for perverting Justice, by those who call evil good and good evil. 

Right judgment doesn't mean weak judgment. Or lack of judgment. We should consider the poor with the understanding that it's often sin that brings them to poverty. That we should provide the opportunity for gleaning our fields. To be generous and hospitable, while maintaining the biblical truth; *that if any would not work, neither should he eat.* 

We should plead for the fatherless, explicitly standing against the causes of fatherlessness, such as fornication and adultery and divorce. 

Feeding and clothing the needy is an opportunity to do unto them and by doing so, doing it unto God. What we do to the least of our brothers, we are doing to the Lord. Therefore, it should be personal and local. Acts of charity and alms done discreetly, or by our churches. Not given off to the government to do for us. 

Biblical Christianity, not "social justice."

<!-- post 17435 · 2026-04-28 06:01 · study -->

**Ruth 3:10-11** 
*And he said, Blessed be thou of the Lord, my daughter: for thou hast shewed more kindness in the latter end than at the beginning, inasmuch as thou followedst not young men, whether poor or rich. And now, my daughter, fear not; I will do to thee all that thou requirest: for all the city of my people doth know that thou art a virtuous woman.*

**Proverbs 31:10** asks the rhetorical question; Who can find a virtuous woman? Boaz, in the story of Ruth certainly does. A wife is a gift of God. 

**Proverbs 18:22**
*Whoso findeth a wife findeth a good thing, and obtaineth favour of the Lord.* 

A virtuous wife? Their price is far above rubies. More valuable than gold. A gift from God

Proverbs has instructed women not to be contentious, loud, not striving with their husbands. Not driving their man to the corner of the rooftop, or out into the wilderness by being a nagging continuous dropping, like heavy rain. 

But here, in the final chapter of the sayings of Lemuel's mother, we find God's ideal for a woman. 

She is trustworthy, that her husband never has to worry about adultery. He can safely trust she won't be spoiled by another. She does him good and not evil, every day. 

She is diligent in her ways, seeking materials and food for her family, working with what she's given to provide for them. Rising early, while it's still dark to cook for them. 

A hard worker, who manages a household, starts small businesses, buys land, makes it fruitful and profitable by the strength of her arms. 

She uses her talents to supplement the household income, selling her wares, knowing her own family is taken care of first, seeing them clothed against winter. 

She helps the poor, is generous and hospitable. 

**1 Timothy 3:11**
*Even so must their wives be grave, not slanderers, sober, faithful in all things.*

Like the pastor's wife, her temperance and chaste behavior is a crown for her husband, increasing his reputation, not shaming him. 

The work of her hands is valued and valuable. She is known by her strength and honor. Her patient attention to these virtues ensure her long-term joy. 

She speaks wisdom and kindness. A blessing to those that hear her. Her children learn her law and the fear of God at her feet. 

She isn't lazy, or idle. 

Paul warns against this tendency in young widows, but it applies to far too many wives;

**1 Timothy 5:13**
*And withal they learn to be idle, wandering about from house to house; and not only idle, but tattlers also and busybodies, speaking things which they ought not.*

This is in stark contrast with the hard working, wise and kind virtuous woman.

*Her children arise up, and call her blessed; her husband also, and he praiseth her.* *Many daughters have done virtuously, but thou excellest them all.*

The mother and wife who is the joy of her children and husband doesn't become this way by accident or by virtue of being married and having children, but by her following the way of the Lord in all things. 

Favor is deceitful; by vain words and flattery, she may seem to be virtuous, because of her beauty in her youth, be attractive and alluring. But these things don't last. *But a woman who fears the Lord, she shall be praised*. 

*Give her of the fruit of her hands; and let her own works praise her in the gates.*

Allow a virtuous woman to be rewarded for her enterprise and let her be praised among men for her value.

**Proverbs 19:14**
*House and riches are the inheritance of fathers: and a prudent wife is from the LORD.*

## Proverbs 31  ·  Series study (other pass)  ·  2025-02-03

**Proverbs 31**
*The words of king Lemuel, the prophecy that his mother taught him.*

*2 What, my son? and what, the son of my womb? and what, the son of my vows?*

*3 Give not thy strength unto women, nor thy ways to that which destroyeth kings.*

*4 It is not for kings, O Lemuel, it is not for kings to drink wine; nor for princes strong drink:*

*5 Lest they drink, and forget the law, and pervert the judgment of any of the afflicted.*

*6 Give strong drink unto him that is ready to perish, and wine unto those that be of heavy hearts.*

*7 Let him drink, and forget his poverty, and remember his misery no more.*

*8 Open thy mouth for the dumb in the cause of all such as are appointed to destruction.*

*9 Open thy mouth, judge righteously, and plead the cause of the poor and needy.*

*10 Who can find a virtuous woman? for her price is far above rubies.*

*11 The heart of her husband doth safely trust in her, so that he shall have no need of spoil.*

*12 She will do him good and not evil all the days of her life.*

*13 She seeketh wool, and flax, and worketh willingly with her hands.*

*14 She is like the merchants' ships; she bringeth her food from afar.*

*15 She riseth also while it is yet night, and giveth meat to her household, and a portion to her maidens.*

*16 She considereth a field, and buyeth it: with the fruit of her hands she planteth a vineyard.*

*17 She girdeth her loins with strength, and strengtheneth her arms.*

*18 She perceiveth that her merchandise is good: her candle goeth not out by night.*

*19 She layeth her hands to the spindle, and her hands hold the distaff.*

*20 She stretcheth out her hand to the poor; yea, she reacheth forth her hands to the needy.*

*21 She is not afraid of the snow for her household: for all her household are clothed with scarlet.*

*22 She maketh herself coverings of tapestry; her clothing is silk and purple.*

*23 Her husband is known in the gates, when he sitteth among the elders of the land.*

*24 She maketh fine linen, and selleth it; and delivereth girdles unto the merchant.*

*25 Strength and honour are her clothing; and she shall rejoice in time to come.*

*26 She openeth her mouth with wisdom; and in her tongue is the law of kindness.*

*27 She looketh well to the ways of her household, and eateth not the bread of idleness.*

*28 Her children arise up, and call her blessed; her husband also, and he praiseth her.*

*29 Many daughters have done virtuously, but thou excellest them all.*

*30 Favour is deceitful, and beauty is vain: but a woman that feareth the Lord, she shall be praised.*

*31 Give her of the fruit of her hands; and let her own works praise her in the gates.*
